# Compatibility gap map (Unity 6000.0.71f1)

What Unbound does not yet do as the Editor does, found by auditing the engine itself rather than
waiting for a game to hit it. `ROADMAP.md` says what is done and probed; this file lists what is
left, ranked, so work can go down it.

Audit of 2026-10-29: read-only, every subsystem; nothing was measured against the Editor, so each
row still needs a probe before and after it is fixed. Paths are relative to `managed/UnityEngine/`
unless they start with `crates/` or `native/`.

## Known insignificant residuals

By the user's decision of 2026-11-01, small differences with no visible effect or
game-logic change do not block package admission. Raw values and comparator
tolerances stay unchanged; strict verifiers still report these failures.
Roughly 1-3/255 in isolated pixels is an example, not a blanket tolerance.
Visible or functional systematic biases, wrong functional answers, changes in
survival, death/event timing or ordering, and actual bugs remain actionable.
A numeric lifetime-field difference alone does not establish a functional change;
its effect on the measured histories still matters. No Native captures or deep research are spent solely
to eliminate a listed insignificant residual.

| humanoid / 6000.0.71f1 | `blendTransition.step18.body` pivotWeight, pivotPosition; `blend*.step5`, `blendTransition.step24`, `blendAdditive.step17`, `mask*.step5`/`step17`, `maskOverride{UpperRoot,NoFootIK}.step16`, `additiveTree.step5`/`step17`, `settings*.step4`/`step5`/`step17`, `settingsFeetRight.step11` `.motion` velocity; `stateMirror*`, `additiveMirror`, `transitionMirror`, `ik{,Clip}Mirror` `.step5`/`step10`/`step11`/`step14`/`step15`/`step17` `.motion` velocity (mirrored states, the same small steps); `exit*`, `crossFadeFixed`, `crossFadeOffset`, `playState`, `events`, `eventsMirror`, `additiveMirrorClip`, `longState`, `longStateB`, `emptyExit`, `emptyCond`, `emptyBack` `.motion` velocity on the same kind of small steps; `settingsBakeOffset.step15`, `settingsCycle.step23` `.body` pivotPosition[0] (4.6e-4 of 1.86, 4.0e-4 of 0.42, pivotWeight matching); `ik`, `ikB`, `ikMirror`, `ikClipMirror` `.pose` muscles[41]/[43] (left Arm Twist In-Out and Forearm Twist In-Out) on most steps | pivotWeight `0.4642` versus `0.4632` and pivotPosition up to `0.056` (of about 31) at the update where the clip's motion reverses (both feet slips about 1e-7); velocity components up to 3.8e-4 absolute (7e-3 relative on a component of about 0.055 beside one of about 52) where the step is small; muscles 41 and 43 up to 0.0108 (0.0028 on `ikB`), equal and opposite, always a whole multiple (1 to 3) of 0.0036 (HumanA) or 0.00093 (HumanB), with every transform's locals matching | Float precision: the pivot weight is the ratio of two near-zero slips there, and the next update matches again; velocity is the small delta over the delta time; the two settings pivotPositions differ by about 4e-4 on their x component, which is small beside the others (about 26), with the pivot weight matching. No effect on the root's path (rootPosition, deltaPosition match). Measured 2026-11-06 (Unbound traced against the reference): (1) velocity: the deltaPosition errors are about one float step of the clip-space root motion (values about 1, 1.2e-7) times the root's scale 100 and humanScale 1.05, about 1.25e-5 world, of either sign and without bias (whole steps on the large forward z component, fractions of one on x and y); velocity multiplies them by 30, and whichever component is small beside the others fails the relative check (y about 0.9 beside z about 40, or z on `blendTransition.step24`, `settingsFeetRight.step11` and `stateMirrorB`). (2) pivot: at `blendTransition.step18` both feet slips are 1.4e-7 and 1.6e-7 (clip units, a few float steps of the foot positions), so their ratio is rounding; at `settingsBakeOffset.step15`/`settingsCycle.step23` pivotWeight differs by 7.7e-6/1.6e-5 (within tolerance) and the feet are about 60 world units apart, giving 4e-4 on pivotPosition[0]. (3) arm twist: `HumanPoseHandler.GetHumanPose` removes the forearm's y rotation by turning the upper arm, stopping at the first iteration whose angle grows (under 1 degree). For these IK poses the angle does not converge: it stays at 0.6496 degrees, moving by float noise (about 1e-5 degrees) each iteration, while each iteration moves one 0.0036 step of twist from the forearm to the upper arm. Forcing 1 to 5 iterations reproduces Unity's value at every one of the 116 steps; which count Unity stops at depends on the last bits. In the Editor too, nearly the same looped pose (`ik.step3` and `step27`, `ikB.step0`/`26`, `3`/`27`, `6`/`28`, the other 93 muscles within 1.5e-5) reads back one to three steps apart. No visible effect in the probe (the transforms match; the two muscles' sum matches); a game that writes the read pose back with SetHumanPose gets the twist split between upper arm and forearm differently by up to that much, as the Editor's own spread does. No systematic cause: recorded, not fixed. Since Humanoid transform keys take the single-precision frame times (2026-11-11) the same three families sit on partly other keys: velocity also on `crossFade`, `exit020`, `mixer.clipB`, `mixer.weight1`, `settingsBakeOrig`, `settingsOrigRot` and `stateMirrorB` steps, pivotPosition[0] on `eventsMirrorCycle.step8`, `stateMirrorCycle` and `transitionMirror` in place of `settingsBakeOffset.step15`, the arm twist on other `ik*` steps; five leg-muscle values of the Foot IK family newly exceed the tolerance (`stream.ik.step10` and `stream.ikPass.step10` muscles[35] by 2.4e-3 and muscles[33] by 8.1e-4, `stream.bodyLocal.step24` muscles[31] by 3.4e-4: the sizes the family has on its other steps) while eight listed ones match; they count as differences, the list in `known.json` only shrinks. |

The rise from 599 to 601 was approved by the user on 2026-11-11 with that switch (tests/projects/humanoid has thirteen fewer differing values with it) and is part of the suite baseline since the next full run was promoted. | humanoid / 6000.0.71f1 | `stream.stance` `.pose` bodyPosition and leg, hand and arm muscles (61 values in `known.json`, `stance-drift`); `stream.ik`, `stream.ikPass`, `stream.bodyLocal` `.pose` leg muscles[31], [33], [35] on single steps (`foot-ik-legs`); `bone.rotateThenSolve`, `bone.solveTwice` arm roll muscles[41], [43], the upper arm's and hand's local rotations and left foot muscles[26], [27] (`readback-solve-straight-limbs`) | Stance: the read-back body up to 3.3e-3 (the hips' world position up to 3.6e-3 on a character 105 units tall, 3.4e-5 of its height), leg muscles up to 1.5e-2 (a straight leg's twist; a foot's up-down 2.5e-3), hand and arm muscles up to 9e-4, within the 29 updates recorded. Foot IK legs: up to 2.4e-3 on one step. Read-back solve: arm roll muscles up to 9e-3 (their sum within 9e-5), left foot muscles up to 1e-3 | Measured 2026-11-11, one rig and one clip, with every transform recorded in Editor captures outside the reference. (1) Stance (a job resets the stream to the stance pose each update, the state's Foot IK then pulls the straight legs to a walk's foot goals, root motion applied): the Editor's values depend on how far root motion has moved the Animator from the origin; Unbound's muscles do not, and its stream body about seven times less. Kept at the origin (`stream.stanceInPlace`, in the reference) the two agree on every value. With root motion the muscles the Editor's job reads right after the reset already step as the root moves (the left upper leg's front-back 0.5996863 to 0.5996884 in updates 0 to 3, 0.5997035 in update 4, 0.5996372 in 5 to 7 and 0.5997034 from 8; 0.5996863 on every update in place, 0.5996861 in Unbound), its stream body after the reset wanders over 2.5e-4 where it is constant in place (Unbound's over 3.4e-5), and in the final pose the hips' local position is off Unbound's by about (0, 1.3e-6, -0.5e-6) of the root's local units in updates 5 to 7, about (0, 6e-6, 1.5e-6) in 13 to 20 and (0, -3.5e-5, -5.9e-6) from 21 on (times 100 in the world; under 5e-7 in the other updates), the arms' rotations by up to 0.038 degrees and the legs' by up to 0.047; the hips and the arms sit on runs of updates with steps between them, the legs change every update from 21 on. Every step starts at the update where the root's world z first passes a power of two: 2 at update 4, 4 at 5, 8 at 8, 16 at 13 and 32 at 21; each step so far is larger than the last, and the root is 46 world units from the origin by update 28. Unbound's hips follow a smooth, symmetric path over the cycle. That is the mark of single-precision loss in the Editor's reset away from the origin, the family of the long-clip row below, rather than of a missing Foot IK mechanism; what exactly produces the steps is not known, and the size beyond update 28 or further from the origin, and an Animator placed away from the origin without root motion, are not measured. Also ruled out: rounding of the foot goals (moving them by one single-precision step changes Unbound's result by at most 3e-7 on the body and 1e-4 on a muscle; measured once, runs not kept), the stabilize-feet path (off here), the pivot weight (equal within 6e-6, no event at those updates). (2) Foot IK legs after a job changed the pose: moving the foot goals by one single-precision step changes Unbound's own result by the same 2.4e-3 on the same value (`stream.ik.step10` muscles[35]) and by no more than 6e-6 when the job only reads; the difference is consistent with amplified rounding of a nearly straight leg (measured once, runs not kept). Since Humanoid transform keys take the single-precision frame times the family sits on other steps (five values counted as differences, one left in `known.json`). (3) A solve after the job read the pose back on the root scaled by 100 (`bone.rotateThenSolve`, `bone.solveTwice`, 96 values in `known.json`, `readback-solve-straight-limbs`, recorded with the macOS Editor): the goal is out of reach of the re-based skeleton in both (the body displaced toward the root alike) and the left arm straight; the upper arm and forearm roll muscles split one roll between them, and Unbound's split is off the Editor's in steps of 1.8e-3 (up to 9e-3) that cancel in their sum within 9e-5, with the upper arm's and hand's local rotations following; moving the goal by one single-precision step moves Unbound's own split by 5.4e-3 (step 16, measured once, runs not kept): amplified rounding of a straight arm. The left foot's muscles 26 and 27 are off by up to 1e-3 on five steps of each control, not traced further. |
| humanoid / 6000.0.71f1 | `longClipA`, `longClipB` `.pose` muscles[21]–[36] (the legs) on every step; `.body`/`.motion` x of the root, body and pivot | Leg muscles up to 0.37; the root's x after the first evaluation's 70000 cycles 3.405 versus 3.378 (of a z of 2800010), then the same offset on every step, every step's motion and the spine and arm muscles matching | A humanoid playable 2.8e6 units from the origin: the Editor's leg muscles there drift from the clip's own pose (which Unbound keeps, and the Editor plays near the origin, `playA`); hypothesis, not measured: its foot placement loses float precision that far out. The measured zero `unity_IndirectSpecColor` bug is fixed, including reflection
intensity and Gamma encoding. The light-probe image now matches all 57600 pixels
exactly. Its earlier 56 introduced pixels were fixed, not waived as insignificant.

## Failing probes by cause (triage of 2026-11-11)

Every probe that failed in the sheriff's full suite on `a6f0d8af2` (148 pass / 45 fail / 10 report
of 203) and had no open task, with what its failures are and where they are recorded. Counts are
that run's, except for the named follow-up replays in each row, including
Q369/Q296/Q363/Q248 and Q372/Q368/Q353. One probe can hold several causes; each is listed with its own count. Classes:

- **order**: an order or text Unity's documentation leaves open (see the notes under the table);
- **residual**: listed in [Known insignificant residuals](#known-insignificant-residuals);
- **gap**: a real difference, with the row of the map that carries it;
- **probe**: a defect of the probe, its recipe or the way its reference was recorded.

Corrected references are identified below. Comparators and tolerances are unchanged, and raw
engine differences still fail. The shared sheriff baseline has not been promoted by this work.

The 2026-11-12 lifecycle and scripting follow-ups close inherited Edit-mode execution and startup ownership
(`editinheritance`, 171/171 values), imported texture flags (`assetclones`, 14340/14340),
Editor/Play exit controls (`editorcallbacks`, 114/114 values across four quit profiles),
internal reflection/particle-light root ownership (`hiddenroots`, 15/15),
public flag transitions (`hiddenflags`, 549/549), and material reload identity
(`droppedparams`, 252/252; `materialalias`, 62/62), and captured exception output
(`stackcapture`, 15/15; `testrunner`, all 11 PlayMode nodes).
CRT scheduling now matches all 448 customrt values and 228 images, with 229
additional load-cycle controls in crtload. Created-clip memory matches 81 controls
and closes five appfacts values.
The retained callback order, queued-load timing, stack-utility text, input,
importer and numerical differences below remain visible in the strict comparisons.
Each gap row gives the measured context and the remaining follow-up scope.
The implementation follow-ups are shared serialized engine-property bindings,
host keyboard configuration delivery and layout refresh, general game float
evaluation, and the remaining memory and stack-utility coverage. Exact async
completion frames and cross-object callback order retain their scoped records.

The Q364/Q360 follow-up closes the measured 6000.0.71f1 physics causes above.
The final targeted regression set has 18 passing probes and the retained twod
rendering failure. Physics2ddynamics matches 27676/27676 values, geometryframe2d
50863/50863 and query2dparity 58603/58603, including all 55251 queries. Added
Editor controls preserve every previous scenario dictionary; the query2dparity
and twod references are unchanged. Comparators and tolerances remain unchanged,
and numerical differences within those tolerances remain in the reports.

Open scopes remain explicit. The twod re-sort image needs a rendering follow-up;
its collider.runtime scenario passes. Other Unity releases retain the diagnosed
legacy contact-payload, generated-geometry and cast-witness policies described in
the map. Edge controls cover nonfinite segment metadata and queries, not arbitrary
nonfinite dynamic contacts or malformed adjacent endpoints. The measured results
do not establish universal parity outside the recorded contexts.

The Q401 navigation work reports `hasPath` and `steeringTarget` from the corners
found after each step (reviewed landing on main 05d1946874). The navlinks reference
falls from 374 to 325 differing values and avoidance from 34 configurations in four
groups to 3 in two, with no previously passing value or record lost. The navmesh,
navbaked, navcost and navpath outputs are unchanged. The engine runs were made on
macOS arm64 against the existing Linux Editor references; the Linux land gate gives
the same counts. No new Editor capture was recorded.

Earlier work (Q385) took runtime mesh regions first, then neighbour prediction, link
movement and reporting, query/height scope and closure, each in its own reviewed landing.
The original translated-build differences fell from 36 to zero, with all nine bounds
controls matching, and the restored-baked-mesh agent failures from 238 to 212 frame
scenarios. All 1453 added navlinks orientation, endpoint, stopping and transform-write
records and all 608 values in 44 added navmesh prediction, finite-input and
large-obstacle scenarios match.

The six-probe regression gate retains strict raw failures: navmesh 212 frame
scenarios, navlinks 325 values, 3 avoidance configurations, navpath placed routes
23/163, wide-link offsets 2/167 and post avoidance 17/578, and navbaked height agents
14/816 (navpath `carve.rules` 2/551 are recorded in the carving row). Imported height queries remain 103/103; runtime height generation remains
unsupported with its warning. `replan.topofar` is closed at 1687/1687. The separate
stacked-link diagnostic fails strictly in 523 values across 18 scenarios and remains a
topology follow-up. Its reference does not replace the main navpath reference or admit
any failures in that comparator.

Remaining work is split by observable cause. Two residuals turn on ties that rounding
decides: walker's desired ray through the vertex where the next edge starts (navmesh,
211 frames) and the `endatlink` link end one float step from the path's end (navlinks
corners of frames 1 to 81). `areamask` desired velocity differs from 102 after a
sub-millimetre trajectory difference from frame 6 (54; a flipped sample choice is a
hypothesis). The three avoidance configurations' second-frame steering target is
Unity's intermediate corner at x = 42.67, which the agent's line to its destination
passes 2e-5 to 7e-5 m away; the cause is open. The others are the `crosstile` approach direction
after the occupied link frees (118); the disabled occupied link's path reports (59);
baked-agent corners (blocked), arrival (right) and hasPath (left); bent and wide-link
query routes; persistent point-link layer ownership and selective reconnection; bump/ramp
arrival; runtime height generation; and post avoidance. Q401 rejected two candidate
rules that regressed measured cases: merging only exactly equal straight-path points
(no change) and an arrival test against the previous step's straight distance (navlinks
325 -> 501; avoidance arrival, arrival-start and runs each fail 2 configurations). The three climber rounding
records stay in the raw count and the numerical-residual table, without a corrective
constant. Other releases retain the previous unmeasured navigation mechanisms
through Compat. No capture establishes universal parity outside its recorded context.

### Order and text Unity's documentation leaves open

- **Order between GameObjects.** Manual 6000.0, *Order of execution for event functions*,
  Limitations: "In general, you can't rely on the order in which the same event function is invoked
  for different GameObjects, except when the order is explicitly documented or settable", and the
  `Update` of one MonoBehaviour "might be called before or after the Update function for the same
  MonoBehaviour on another GameObject, including its own parent or child GameObjects". The
  activationorder keys, the 24 editorcallbacks events and 59 oroorder lines are such orders
  (`OnRenderObject`'s page says only that it "is called after camera has rendered the Scene", on
  "any object that has a script with the function"). In each of them the two sides hold the same
  events with the same payloads; each object's own Awake, OnEnable, Start sequence and the
  configurable script execution order are kept. What is not shown: that no game depends on the
  Editor's order. A game that does would behave differently, as it could between two Editor
  releases; the raw sequences stay in the reports.
- **Exception message text.** The exceptions are of the documented type; their messages end with
  the parameter name in the form of the runtime Unbound runs on (`... (Parameter 'name')`) where
  the Editor's runtime writes it on a second line (`Parameter name: name`); one
  `NullReferenceException` message ends with a full stop only in Unbound. No Unity page documents
  the text. A game that parses it would see the difference.

### Probe and reference repairs

- **Recording mode, Q363.** `destroycontract`, `activationorder` and `shaderpasscount`
  now use two identical windowed GPU Editor captures from the existing display. The
  [Unity 6000.0 EOF limitation](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/WaitForEndOfFrame.html)
  excludes batch-mode execution. Their READMEs state every changed row scope.
  Destroycontract still fails 887 keys because first-render/EOF startup differs in
  this fixture; re-recording did not establish engine parity.
- **Numeric recorder, Q363.** Oroorder now names only explicitly formatted instance
  IDs. Two Editor repeats preserve every reference line. Its 23 corrupted numeric
  payload pairs are resolved; the other 91 missing/extra pairs remain raw.
- **TextCore recipe and counter, Q369.** The font recipe already copies and verifies
  both fonts, so all 372 cases run. The suite now reads `N of M values differ` and
  counts the existing 8226 failures. S19 stored zero counted failures because its
  parser missed that form, and its missing-font recipe compared no cases. A replay
  against that unchanged baseline therefore flags the visibility increase. It is
  not waived and no baseline is promoted here.
- **Wind clock, Q363.** A fixed capture interval replaces wall-time-dependent
  phases. All 18 records match, with the same raw comparator and tolerances.
- **Work-area sampling, Q296.** Inputplatform waits up to ten seconds for both
  changed and restored work areas. A 30-frame delayed control passes 11/11; the
  ten-frame sampler fails that control at `workArea.changed`. The normal control
  passes 43/43 and the undelayed work-area control 11/11. All five Editor reference
  files remain byte-identical.
- **Stopwatch units, Q248.** The new Linux Editor reference has 12 records from two
  identical windowed GPU captures, including an escaping readonly field reference.
  The original runtime matches 6/12, the corrected one 12/12. Engine-internal timer
  units are unchanged; the game-assembly members use the measured 100 ns unit.

## How gaps hide

Game runs keep hitting features that are missing *without a warning* (physical cameras,
`Camera.SetReplacementShader`). The generated stubs warn on first use; these do not:

| Pattern | Example | Why it is silent |
|---|---|---|
| Hand-written empty body | `Camera.SetReplacementShader { }`, `Animator.ApplyBuiltinRootMotion` | A hand-written member suppresses the generated stub, and with it the warning (516 empty public bodies in the hand-written sources, many legitimate) |
| Stored, never consumed | `Physics2D.simulationLayers` | Auto-properties and fields the frame/simulation never reads |
| Serialized field or import setting not read | `m_DisabledShaderPasses`, `m_RenderingPath`, terrain lightmap, ModelImporter clip masks/root motion, `m_ContactPairsMode` | `ReadSerialized` / the importers read a subset of keys and drop the rest |
| Message looked up, never sent | `OnParticleUpdateJobScheduled` (`Messages.cs` `s_NotSent` lists them) | Messages are not API calls: `api-gaps` cannot see them |
| Overload drops a parameter | `Graphics.ConvertTexture(src, srcElement, dst, dstElement)`, `Mesh.Set*(..., flags)`, stereo-eye overloads (Blit slices, `LogOption`, `SetPropertyBlock` index done) | Forwarded to the simpler overload |
| Asset class with a ClassId but no reader | done (`classids`): a class without a reader warns once at load, naming the class and the file, and `unbound doctor` lists the project's files holding one | Ignored classes (`ClassCoverage.s_Ignored`, with a reason) stay silent |
| Stub default not neutral | done (`stubdefaults`): value-returning stubs take the Editor's answer from `StubDefaults.cs` where it is a constant; `ScalableBufferManager` and `CalculateObliqueMatrix` are hand-written, `Microphone.devices` takes the generator's empty-array rule | Members whose answer is not a constant still return the default after their warning (see the tooling item) |
| Generated stub events | done: subscribing warns that the event is never raised | |
| Claimed capability | `SystemInfo.supportsTextureWrapMirrorOnce` (MirrorOnce samples as Mirror) | Packages enable a path that then draws nothing |
| Warn once, then silent | SpriteMask, reflection probes | A second scene shows nothing in the log |

## Tooling first: make every gap loud

Each of these turns a class of silent gaps into a list that keeps itself up to date:

- [x] Q403 declaration checking: `unbound api-check` compares release metadata, including custom
      attribute arguments, AttributeUsage, optional defaults, constants, modifiers, constraints,
      interfaces, accessors and storage metadata. See [declaration policy](API_DECLARATIONS.md).
      The current 6000.0.71f1 audit has 407 exact migration exceptions in
      `managed/UnityEngine/ApiCheckAllowList.json`: attributes 246, modifiers 13, accessors 4,
      layout 68, interfaces 64, constraints 1 and eleven lookup policies. These remain open
      declaration repairs, with raw counts and individual reasons retained by the checker.
      Q403 lookup checks are done (`attributeinheritance`, 215 exact Editor records): thirty
      attribute-type declarations now match the release metadata; eleven direct-discovery lookup
      policies carry individual measured reasons. TypeCache, editor registration and JSON rename/
      envelope controls match the Editor; serialized prefab renames remain supported.

ParticleSystem resolves 337 more, Editor resolves 132 and UIElements resolves 61 more across its values and shared JSON string transfer. - [x] ClassId coverage: Unity's class-ID table against registered readers; a ClassId without a
      reader fails loudly instead of default-constructing. Done (`classids`: Unity 6000.0.71f1's
      own class table; `Runtime/ClassCoverage.cs`): a document whose class has no engine class, or

      EditorExtensionImpl, and OcclusionCullingSettings while it references no baked data

      73398921, `VisualEffectResource` 2058629511), `Preset` (181963792; editor scripts can apply
      one, explicitly unsupported). Legacy `GUILayer` (92) is discarded on load in the measured
      6000.0.71f1 release, so its doctor entry is ignored (`classids` legacy control). AudioListener
      and FlareLayer have only the enabled flag read by the shared Behaviour reader and are not
      listed as missing readers. FlareLayer adds a required Camera, repairs a missing Camera on
      load with the Editor's warning, and retains disabled Cameras (`flarelayerdependency`,
      Unity 6000.0.71f1). Doctor reports missing/disabled Camera dependencies. Importer `.meta`
      and `ProjectSettings/*.asset` unread leaf paths now use the metadata/startup readers and
      generated physics reader declarations, with JSON and human totals. Unread options remain
      "may change behaviour", including options that affect imported data; each no-runtime-effect
      exception carries a reason. Field consumption does not establish full feature parity.
      The Camera probe controls asynchronous Editor shader compilation through ShaderUtil;
      the first dark explicit frame in the original observation follows shader readiness, not a
      fixed Camera initialization delay. Other releases' missing-Camera load repair fails
      explicitly as unmeasured. Editor asynchronous shader-startup timing remains a follow-up.
      Bundle-scene load failures point to doctor. YAML `Sprite` documents (213)
      now read ordinary un-atlased geometry and references (`spriteassets`); rejected layouts
      are errors at load and listed by `unbound doctor`. `LightingSettings` (850595691) and
      `ShaderVariantCollection` (200) read their data (`lightingassets`): the scenes' Lighting
      Settings back `UnityEditor.Lightmapping` (bake inputs: they change no Play mode frame);
      collections answer Unity's counts, queries and warm-up state. The `ShaderVariant` constructor
      validates explicit per-pass keyword choices, including global/local `multi_compile`,
      `shader_feature`, dynamic branches and placeholders (`lightingassets variant-sets`).
      Built-in shorthand combination restrictions, serialized built-in, surface and fixed-function
      pass choices remain unmeasured and retain prior validation. Collection WarmUp and progressive
      warm-up prepare GPU programs and pipelines for the current attachments and render state
      (`lightingassets` cold GPU checks), with no extra program/pipeline creation on the covered
      first draws. Empty full warm-up and saturating progressive counts match the Editor. Inherited pass
      preparation follows the same fallback traversal as shadow drawing (`lightingassets fallback`).
      Global Shader.WarmupAllShaders uses the same GPU preparation for every loaded shader's
      source variants, across SubShaders and fallbacks, without changing collection progress
      (`lightingassets all`, Q219). Source groups are enumerated lazily with excluded variants
      omitted. Mixed float/signed/unsigned programs prepare during startup too; incompatible
      attachment types defer only their pipelines. Later attachment/layout/state changes can still
      require driver work, as documented for Vulkan/Metal/DX12. Unreadable source programs fail explicitly.
- [ ] `SystemInfo` answers follow what the renderer does.
- [ ] `api-gaps` noise: trivial attributes (`JetBrains.Annotations.*`, `ResourcePathAttribute`,
      `CategoryInfoAttribute`, `ShaderKeywordFilter.*`) implemented, so the coverage line counts
      real gaps; `InitializeOnLoad*` (honoured by name) stops showing as missing.
- [ ] Probes where there are none: `animator` (blend trees, layers, root motion), `audio` (mixer,
      filters, through `GetOutputData`), lifecycle messages (mouse, focus/pause, visibility,
      reparenting).

## The map

**S** = silent (no warning), **W** = warns (stub or "skipped"), impact **H**/M/L on real games.

### Core runtime and messages

Done: lifecycle and execution order, all `RuntimeInitializeLoadType`s, `[InitializeOnLoad*]`,
coroutines (measured timing), SendMessage family, parent/children/canvas messages, Transform API,
Instantiate, scenes (single/additive/async, events, merge/move), serialization (SerializeReference,
callbacks, FormerlySerializedAs), prefab variants/nesting/overrides, Time, PlayerPrefs.

The existing implementation preserves the measured equal-priority FIFO completion, progress and activation hold; exact timing remains open, with all strict failures visible. Unity 6000.0 documents a shared main-thread integration budget for background loading, but Application.backgroundLoadingPriority has no effect in the Editor. AsyncOperation.priority can reorder work that has not started, so FIFO is only the measured equal-priority scope; non-default priorities remain unmeasured and unsupported by this queue. The driver initializes its frame counter in Start, so the template logs -1 or 0 according to which Start runs first; that order is unspecified. Both deferred-Destroy OnDisable order lines now match. | A coroutine waiting for an AsyncOperation resumes at the next Update's coroutine point | `Runtime/Coroutines.cs` (Wait, Resume), `Core/ScriptableObject.cs` (AsyncOperation.Complete) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navbaked `resume.*`: LoadSceneAsync additive, held by allowSceneActivation, UnloadSceneAsync, Resources.LoadAsync, InstantiateAsync; managed CoroutineTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, AsyncOperation): it can be yielded from a coroutine; when the coroutine resumes is not documented. Measured: the first coroutine that yielded a running operation resumes as the operation completes (in EarlyUpdate.UpdatePreloading for scene loads and unloads and Resources loads, UpdateAsyncInstantiate for InstantiateAsync), after sceneLoaded and before the completed handlers, so before the frame's early AI update; another coroutine yielding the same operation, and one yielding an operation already done, resumes at the next Update's coroutine point. Unmeasured, given the same rule: UnityWebRequest operations (they complete where Unbound's main-thread queue drains), AssetBundle loads and unloads, `WaitForCompletion` (the waiting coroutine resumes inside the call), NavMeshBuilder.UpdateNavMeshDataAsync, a coroutine stopped while waiting (the next one to yield the operation takes its place). Applies to every release; only 6000.0.71f1 is measured. Scene-load staging is now measured by `editorcallbacks/SceneLoop`: `Compat.SceneAsyncPreparationMeasured` separates document preparation from activation, serializes queued integrations, holds the FIFO at 0.9, and preserves preloading completion and coroutine resumption. The measured small first/later single and additive loads complete in frame 2; held loads activate after release, and synchronous loads force their pending prefix in the next frame. This is not a duration guarantee for arbitrary content. Other releases retain the previous one-pass timing, unmeasured there. |
| Pending Start-only scripts run at the end of LateUpdate | `Runtime/Lifecycle.cs`, `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (`editorcallbacks/SceneLoop --starts-only`, 10 identical Editor records, and managed StartPhaseTests): pending scripts created/enabled by a coroutine or LateUpdate start at PostLateUpdate.ScriptRunDelayedDynamicFrameRate; a script with LateUpdate still starts at its callback visit. Startup and Update scripting points retain their existing drains. `Compat.PendingStartsAfterLateUpdate` selects this measured leaf; other releases retain the prior LateUpdate-end drain until measured. |
| `WaitForSeconds`, `Awaitable.WaitForSecondsAsync` and `Invoke` come due at the wrong point (a zero wait started during Update resumed in that frame, before the next frame's fixed steps; an Invoke ran only after Update) | `Runtime/Coroutines.cs` (FrameTimedWait, Invokes), `Runtime/Awaitable.cs`, `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs` | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/waitforseconds: 75 values, two identical windowed GPU Editor captures; managed TimedWaitTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, WaitForSeconds): the coroutine continues "on the first frame after 't' seconds has passed". Measured: both timed waits count from the time of the frame that starts them (also from a fixed step, where Time.time reads the fixed clock) and end at the first Update coroutine point of a later frame whose time has reached the end, so a zero or negative wait never ends in its own frame; a long frame does not delay the start (the page's "start waiting at the end of the current frame" is not what a 250 ms frame shows). Invoke timing is additionally measured by tests/projects/invokesteps: the first deadline uses the earlier of the fixed and frame clocks, the fixed-step check reads that step's clock, and checks also run after Update and LateUpdate. A single late repeat runs once per check and keeps its original cadence; deadline-ordered traversal can revisit interleaved repeats. A one-shot is removed before its callback; cancelled neighbors are excluded from subsequent traversal, while cancellation inside a callback can expose an already-reinserted repeat. NaN deadlines remain pending without callbacks (22 values in invokesteps, two identical captures). These additional rules use Compat for 6000.0.71f1; other releases retain the previous, unmeasured clock and delayed points. Deferred: a delay that ends exactly on a frame boundary (the Editor resumed 1 s at 0.125 s per frame after 8 frames in one capture and 9 in the next). Unmeasured: timeScale changes during a wait, custom player loops without these points. The timed-wait frame policy applies to every release; only 6000.0.71f1 is measured. |
| Manual `Object.Destroy` request state and deferred callbacks | `Runtime/Instantiation.cs`, `Runtime/Lifecycle.cs`, `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs` | S | measured implementation for Unity 6000.0.71f1 in `destroyphases`: 61 controls, 5370 records from identical independent windowed GPU Editor captures. Zero-delay requests disable Behaviours on the target and direct children before returning; delivery consumes the requests present on entry to delayed Fixed/Update/PostLateUpdate scripting points. Requests from Starts, coroutines and OnDestroy wait for the next point. Positive delays use scaled time without early disabling; runtime assets/built-ins use the same queue, persistent assets refuse Destroy. Immediate destruction respects callback restrictions and awakened scripts receive one OnDisable even inside Awake. Async scene unload tears down at a later preloading pass; its elapsed completion time is unspecified. The active loop profile uses the existing destroycontract loop.leaves reference, including internal leaves rather than every public API type. Queue drains and reusable request snapshots allocate nothing after capacity is warm. A particle system's frame update completes (collision/trigger messages, stop callbacks and actions) at PostLateUpdate.ParticleSystemEndUpdateAll, or earlier when a script reads the system after the update began (inferred from destroyphases' own sample; a read of another system completing it is unmeasured), so a system whose Destroy is delivered at PostLateUpdate's scripting point sends no message (particletriggerapi */gone) unless a script read it first (destroyphases' own after.particles sample; trigger controls of other sizes, spaces and colliders); the former particle-specific guard is gone and the comparator checks particle callbacks, ticks and first-frame visibility strictly. MissingReferenceException names the type with its namespace. IK-time destruction matches the animation-end leaf after Q162 (`destroyphases`: 5370/5370 records, no residuals or unexplained differences); the former IK leaf residual is closed. Q374 closes the Q363 windowed `destroycontract` startup EOF discrepancy, 1115/1115 keys now match; see `First rendered frame and EOF coroutine startup`. The batch-mode mismatch is closed. Other releases retain unmeasured fallback timing. Startup pause/focus false/true notifications are measured; OS background pause and post-request re-enabling/new scripts remain outside this capture; the separate focus control below measures window focus transitions. |

| No main-thread checks on engine API | `Runtime/MainThreadCheck.cs`, Core (Object, GameObject, Component, Transform, Time, Random, Application, Resources, SceneManagement), Input, Camera, PlayerPrefs, SystemInfo, TextAsset, Texture2D, Mesh, UnityWebRequest | S | done for the measured members (tests/projects/mainthread, Unity 6000.0.71f1: 590/590 outcomes on the main thread, a Thread, Task.Run, an IJob on a worker and IJob.Run). Members that are not thread-safe throw Unity's `UnityException: <binding> can only be called from the main thread.` (binding names as measured) on other threads and inside any job, IJob.Run included; `GetInstanceID` and a destroyed non-script object's null comparison throw `InvalidOperationException` off the main thread only. Additional measured coverage in tests/projects/threadapis: 1,222 exact outcomes from 134 calls across ordinary threads, async continuations, jobs, animation and two contact-modification contexts, plus loading/creation controls. The replay matches 1,222/1,222 outcomes without allowances. The supplementary followups reference covers Renderer, Animator and AudioSource property guards across five execution contexts, plus material instantiation, controllerless initialization and clipless pending playback lifecycle controls. These component defaults use the measured release profile; other releases retain the previous, unmeasured initialization flags. Representative Rigidbody, Collider, Physics queries, Renderer, Material, Animator, AudioSource, Canvas, RectTransform and Invoke members share the central allocation-free check and preserve each measured binding message. Constructors and field initializers are restricted until creation callbacks begin; IAnimationJob callbacks have job restrictions while animation handles remain usable. ### Built-in render pipeline

Done: forward base/add lighting, vertex/SH lights, shadows (cascades, spot, point; casters from
fallback shaders), camera matrices and physical cameras, clear flags, MSAA, command buffers at
every forward event, OnPre*/OnPost*/OnRenderImage and [ImageEffectOpaque], depth, depth-normals
and motion-vector textures, sorting layers, SortingGroup and transparency sort axes, fog, ambient
modes, lightmaps and light probes, custom, baked and realtime reflection probes with blending/box
projection, keywords, GrabPass, LODGroup, SubShader LOD, disabled passes, `.renderTexture`
assets, Display 1 only, projectors, replacement shaders, oblique mirrors with inverted culling,
cubemap renders, camera skyboxes, deferred and vertex-lit paths, material variants, raster
states (tests/projects/builtinrp, builtinfx, display, baked, lighting).

| Lens flare element edge line | `Graphics/Halos.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/flares, OpenGL Core, NVIDIA: every image without a halo is exact, the `f.*`, `q.*`, `o.*` and the new `fl.*` cases; the `h.*` and `ho.*` images differ only in their halo pixels, by the residual entry; counts in the cause table above; managed FlareGeometryTests). The cause was the construction, not a rasterization rule: Unbound built the elements in viewport pixels with fitted constants (a scale of 0.0100354, image insets by the part of the texture outside an image). They are now quads in view space at the flare's depth, half `size * 0.01` of that depth, a zooming element also times the brightness and visibility, on the line from the flare's point to the view axis, a rotating element 1.4 half sides out with its first corner away from the axis, a directional flare at twice the near plane's distance, each image its cell less half a texel (the large and medium images of the second layout keep their left and top sides whole), with the sums in the one order that reproduces the reference images to the last float bit; the camera's projection alone places them. Documented (Manual 6000.0, Flare asset reference, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/class-Flare.html): an element's Position runs "along a line running from the containing GameObject's position through the screen center", and Rotate turns it towards the centre of the screen. Measured with the `fl.*` cases, and changed by this from what Unbound drew before: under an off-centre projection (a lens shift of a quarter of the width) the Editor's elements follow the view axis, not the screen's centre, so there the documented wording does not hold (one horizontal shift is measured; a vertical one and a script-set off-centre or oblique projection are not); a zooming element is half and twice as large at brightness (or a light's flareStrength) 0.5 and 2; a flare beyond the far plane or nearer than the near plane is not drawn; a directional flare 50 degrees off the view axis is drawn and one 70 degrees off is not. Not measured: the angle at which a directional flare leaves the view (60 degrees in Unbound, from its placement) and its depth under a script-set projection (the camera's near plane is used); whether a flare that is not drawn for these reasons keeps fading in (it does in Unbound); an orthographic camera with a directional flare (not drawn, as before); an element of negative size or behind an orthographic camera (not drawn); the distance from the view axis under which a rotating element stops turning (1e-5 in Unbound; on the axis it points along +x as measured); flares under a render pipeline; drivers other than NVIDIA's |

Documented: the transition height is screen-relative in [0, 1] and `lodBias` multiplies the switching distance (Scripting API 6000.0 [LOD.screenRelativeTransitionHeight](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/LOD-screenRelativeTransitionHeight.html), [QualitySettings.lodBias](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/QualitySettings-lodBias.html)); the tie rule is measured. Unbound's choices, not measured: a group of size 0 and a level of height 0, or `lodBias` 0 with the camera at the reference point, draw nothing. Not modelled (measured on one machine only, probe `run.py editor --hold`, not in the reference): in the Editor a camera rendered again within about 1 ms of real time can draw the levels chosen for its previous place (a 0.5 ms wait drew the old level, 0.75 ms the new one); Unbound chooses at every render. The previous reference was a batch-mode capture with renders that close; windowed captures give the same records with or without the wait. Cross-fades are not drawn (deferred, see below) |

Unmeasured, chosen: a script's Camera.Render() and any drawing on the screen after a stack's resolve (late command buffers, blits, GL) end the stack; deferred cameras after an HDR stack and on explicit targets (whole target) seed their light buffer by the same rule; a full-screen camera keeping the color after a deferred one starts a stack of its own with its own samples; a deferred camera with image effects drops the stack's MSAA as one without. Explicit-target Don't Clear depth is done for initialized retained targets in sequential disabled Camera.Render calls, built-in forward, full rect without effects (`cameraexplicitdepth`, Unity 6000.0.71f1 Linux OpenGL/NVIDIA, Linear/Gamma, 254/254 values and 210/210 images per space): direct byte/half-float targets retain their own depth; HDR-to-byte renders start with fresh intermediate depth independently of unrelated near/far history, and color resolve preserves the owned target's prior depth, with quality/target samples 1/4. Native's clear-versus-replace mechanism is unresolved. Also measured (camerastack, Linear and Gamma, 96 cases x 2 frames each pass pixel for pixel): a color-clearing camera inside a screen stack (full and partial, LDR and HDR, quality AA 0 and 4), a partial camera between full-screen cameras (A LDR and HDR), a perspective deferred stacked camera at quality AA 0 and 4 (`.persp`; the orthographic `.def` cases render Forward): a deferred camera that clears only depth or nothing starts its light buffer from the color under it, a partial screen one renders at its pixel size, and a full-screen one continuing a screen stack makes that stack render without MSAA. Not a parity target: the contents a Don't Clear camera finds in a new, discarded or pooled target, which Unity 6000.0 documents as undefined ([RenderTexture.Create](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/RenderTexture.Create.html), [DiscardContents](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/RenderTexture.DiscardContents.html), [GetTemporary](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/RenderTexture.GetTemporary.html)). Unmeasured, chosen: a screen read between a frame's start and its first screen pass (an offscreen camera's OnPostRender) reads the last frame, as that pass carries it forward. Unmeasured: explicit-target deferred/partial/effect depth history |

| LineRenderer/TrailRenderer geometry | `Graphics/LineBuilder.cs`, `Graphics/LineRenderers.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/lines: 2200/2201 keys, every mesh and image record of lines and trails): one strip builder for every case. Points are taken to a build space (the camera's view space, an orthographic camera's moved forward by its near distance; for TransformZ the transform's axes with z reversed, starting one plus the largest world-bounds half extent back along z from the transform for a line, but forward from the world origin for a trail, which turns the corners and caps of trails near the origin the other way); each segment's side is taken against the direction to the camera (components at least 0.01 from zero, which also gives lines along the view axis a side); short segments (below twice the widest width: the largest width key times the multiplier) pull neighbouring sides together; joints miter where the sides' offset lines cross, faded towards parallel or opposite sides, limited to 3 * length of the segment before the incoming one / (2 * widest width), at least 1; corners are arcs around the inner vertex, caps half discs, both turned the other way behind the camera; width and color are sampled at the travelled fraction (point index for the per-segment texture modes), clamped to [0, 1]. BakeMesh writes world-space lines in world space and local-space ones in local space (useTransform: world orientation without the position); it changes only the TransformZ frame of world-space lines and trails. Drawn strips are written in world space each frame without allocating and drawn with an identity object matrix (lines `img.objectMatrix.*`). A TransformZ segment along the frame's z axis has no side: its joints get NaN vertices as in the Editor (`edge.*.zParallel`). A drawn trail in Static texture mode measures its texture from the path its points travelled (Clear and the expiry of every point restart it); BakeMesh from the head. Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, LineRenderer.BakeMesh): "useTransform: include the rotation and scale of the Transform"; measured (lines `space.*`): it applies to local-space lines, turns only the TransformZ frame of world-space lines and trails, and leaves a world-space View line unchanged. LineRenderer.Simplify and LineUtility.Simplify (lines `simplify.*`): a span keeps its point farthest from the infinite line through its ends when above the tolerance, the first span ends at the last point apart from the first (closed polylines), one point keeps none, LineUtility replaces its output lists. Q374 resolves all 15 `trail.f*.time` values. Open: `line.color.PerceptualBlend` (one byte, the gradient's perceptual sRGB conversion, see particlecurves `perceptual-precision`). Not implemented: shadow bias (Unity moves each point along the build z axis by its width times the bias when drawing shadow casters; Unbound draws shadow casters from the same strip). Gradient key times are 1/65535 steps and a time selects its segment by step (`edge.gradient.closeKeys.*`, particlecurves `gradient/particles/*`). |

| Native rendering plugins: the low-level plugin interface (`UnityPluginLoad`, `IUnityGraphics`, `IUnityLog`, `IUnityProfiler`/`V2`, `IUnityMemoryManager`), `GL.IssuePluginEvent`, `CommandBuffer.IssuePluginEvent*`, `IssuePluginCustomBlit`, `IssuePluginCustomTextureUpdate*`, native texture and render buffer pointers, `CreateExternalTexture`/`UpdateExternalTexture` | `Runtime/NativePlugins.cs`, `Graphics/CommandBuffer.cs`, `crates/unbound-runtime/src/plugin_api.rs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/gl_interop.rs`, `render/executor/plugins.rs` | W | done on Linux for the measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 OpenGL Core cases (tests/projects/nativegfx; tests/projects/cmdbuffers device.plugin.*): load on first call or "Load on startup", callbacks on the main thread in stream order with an OpenGL context sharing the renderer's textures and the current target bound, texture update formats RGBA32/RGB24 (linear and sRGB), custom blit binding and Texture2D-source error, plugin GL errors logged as assertions, two Shutdown events at exit and no unload. a callback between two draws of an executed buffer lands between them; `Graphics.ExecuteCommandBuffer` leaves the active render texture as it was. On the Vulkan device (Q308) `GetRenderer` answers Vulkan and plugins get `IUnityGraphicsVulkan`/`V2` (tests/projects/nativegfx `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/vulkan.jsonl`, 55/61 records, 6 accepted residuals: render texture images without the input attachment usage and multisampled colors without transfer usage, a script's DontCare event outside rendering getting a command buffer; the images of a Cubemap, a Texture2DArray, a Texture3D and a CubemapArray as the Editor's, `plugin.shape.*`): instance, device, queue, recording state per render pass precondition (inside a render pass over the current render texture target, multisampled targets resolved, the same render pass object frame after frame; ended and begun again by EnsureOutside/EnsureInside, kept across an access inside it, ended for later events by an EnsureOutside event), texture (per mip and layer; sRGB formats where the texture is sampled so), render buffer, resolve texture and buffer access with layout barriers, frame counters; not supported (logged): Vulkan function interception, queue access, swapchain configuration, Recreate access, EnsureInside over the screen or several colors (skipped), external textures (`CreateExternalTexture` of an image is refused with an error; unmeasured on the Vulkan Editor); initialization interception answers too late, preloaded plugins included (the device is made before plugins load). On OpenGL Core the plugins' context is in the share group of the renderer's own OpenGL device (Q307), so a native pointer is the renderer's texture itself (a view of it where Unity's device stores it in another format): nothing is moved or copied when a texture is first shared. `IUnityProfilerCallbacks`/`V2` and `IUnityEventQueue` (Q292, tests/projects/nativegfx `profiler.*`, `events.load`, `frames.sequence`; present on Vulkan too, `plugin.interfaces`): category, marker and thread callbacks hear at once of what exists (the Editor's 39 built-in categories with their IDs, names and colors; the markers, categories and threads plugins make; the main thread) and then of each new one (four category and four marker callbacks at a time), marker event callbacks get `EmitEvent` at once whatever records and unregister by marker, by user data or altogether, frame callbacks run when a frame ends (the player loop's `ProfilerEndFrame`), and the queue hands the events sent to it out when the next frame begins (`DispatchEventQueueEvents`), each to the handlers of its ID, the last added first, then to the type's cleanup handler, until none waits; the header's own events add and remove handlers. Its C++ interface and the plugins' handlers are laid out as the Itanium C++ ABI does; the callbacks run without the plugin context's lock, so they may call back into the engine; a custom player loop without those two nodes gets neither (unmeasured). Residuals (`residuals.json`): the Editor's own markers, worker threads and job flows are not modelled (`profiler.editor`). The markers scripts make (`ProfilerMarker`, `Profiler.BeginSample`) are plugins' markers with the script flag (2), heard of by marker callbacks (those made before a plugin loads when its callback registers) and reporting their samples' begin and end to event callbacks (`profiler.script`, exact since Q370 on OpenGL Core; NATIVE_API_VERSION 61); Unbound's own stats (frame times, memory) are not script markers and are not reported. Not measured: `Profiler.BeginSample`, `CustomSampler` and markers made with explicit flags (the same marker path, their flags plus the script flag; the probe records a default `ProfilerMarker`), a single sample (event type 2), a sample's metadata and object context (reported without data), and scripts' counters (`ProfilerCounter`: not reported). `IUnityGraphicsVulkan`/`V2` on the OpenGL Core device are answered as in the Editor: intercepting and swapchain requests answer no (`vulkan.elsewhere`), and what needs the device, which ends the Editor there (`Instance`, `CommandRecordingState`, `ConfigureEvent`, the render pass calls measured), is an error in the script log that gives nothing. `UnityRenderingExtQuery` is asked of loaded plugins with the single-pass stereo question while a built-in pipeline camera renders (three times after OnPreRender, once before OnPostRender and once after: `ext.sequence`, `ext.preload`; measured on one forward camera without shadows, image effects or XR; a plugin's yes changes nothing there, and here it is logged once as of no use; under a render pipeline it is not asked, unmeasured) and `UnityRenderingExtEvent` is not called (the Editor sent none in that run). With no render texture active a plugin finds a framebuffer without attachments and the screen's size as the viewport, as in the batch-mode Editor (`screen.target`). Native pointers of every texture shape (Q292, `shape.*`; OpenGL Core 185/194 records with 9 residuals): a Cubemap's is a cube map, a Texture2DArray's a 2D array whatever its size (one layer, six square ones), a Texture3D's a 3D texture, a CubemapArray's a cube map array, each in the format Unity's device stores it in, and what a plugin writes through the name is the texture's texels; a multisampled render texture's is its resolved image, the depth pointer of one without depth its multisampled color texture, of a cube render texture its color texture; a callback while one face of a cube or one layer of an array render texture is the target finds that image attached (and the depth buffer's) and draws into it alone, while a multisampled target is bound its samples (sRGB texels for an sRGB render texture of a Linear project, as the texture's own name), and what it draws there is resolved. `Texture2D.CreateExternalTexture` of a plugin's own 2D texture (`ext.*`) samples it as OpenGL does, in its own format whatever is declared (`GL_SRGB8_ALPHA8` decoded, `GL_RGBA8` declared sRGB not; measured with `GL_R8`, `GL_RG8`, `GL_RGB8`, `GL_RGBA16F`, `GL_RGBA32F`, DXT5 and the unsized `GL_RGBA`, which the texture reports as it was asked for; by the same rule, unmeasured: the other unsized 8-bit formats (`GL_RED`, `GL_RG`, `GL_RGB`, `GL_SRGB`, `GL_SRGB_ALPHA`), `GL_SRGB8`, the 8-bit signed normalized formats, the unsigned 16-bit normalized ones, `GL_RGB10_A2`, `GL_R11F_G11F_B10F`, the one- to four-channel 16- and 32-bit float formats, and the DXT1, unsigned RGTC, BPTC and unsigned BPTC float blocks), through its mip levels when the external texture has a mip chain and its first level alone otherwise; refused with an error: integer and depth formats, 16-bit signed normalized formats, DXT3, the signed RGTC and signed BPTC float blocks and any other format, block textures of a size that is not whole blocks, and textures that are not 2D. | Graphics devices (Q281): the device a game sees is the one Unbound runs, chosen as the Editor chooses it | `crates/unbound-compat/src/graphics.rs`, `Runtime/GraphicsDeviceProfile.cs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/device.rs` | L | done: OpenGL Core (the Linux Editor's default) and Vulkan, selected by `--force-glcore`/`--force-vulkan`, the project's graphics API list for the OS's standalone platform plus its first default, else the defaults (Linux: OpenGL Core, Vulkan; macOS: Metal, not drawn yet), OpenGL Core last; SystemInfo answers and format tables per device (tests/projects/urp CapsProbe `caps-6000.0.71f1.jsonl`, `unity-6000.0.71f1/caps-vulkan.jsonl`), `SHADER_API_*`, `GL.GetGPUProjectionMatrix` (bit-exact), reversed depth, flipped render-texture projection, top-down screen and the multisampled depth resolve's sample on Vulkan (tests/projects/rtdepth `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/vulkan`: 1071/1071 values, 368/368 images), `IUnityGraphics::GetRenderer`. A built-in pipeline screen camera with command buffers draws into a stand-in render texture stored as the screen is (top-down on Vulkan): a Blit from `CameraTarget`/`CurrentActive` holds the screen's rows as stored, upside down on Vulkan, and wireframe edges rasterize into it as onto the screen (rtdepth `vulkan` `target.screen.*.blit{Active,Cam}.out`, `vulkan/controls` `ctl.wireframe{,Kept}.buffer.final`; a Blit with a material, `CopyTexture` and `ConvertTexture` of it follow the same storage, unmeasured, and `_MainTex_TexelSize.y` keeps its sign: the negative texel height top-down sources have where UV starts at the top is not modelled; a target pairing the stand-in with another texture draws both as the stand-in is stored, unmeasured and warned); Shadow casters' clip-space bias takes the device's clip depth (directional and spot: half, negated; point: half, measured), so the built-in pipeline's shadows match the Vulkan Editor as closely as on OpenGL Core (tests/projects/lighting `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/vulkan`); copies of the top-down screen are raw with their regions counted from the bottom (unmeasured on the Vulkan Editor); on Linux each device is drawn by its own wgpu backend: OpenGL Core by the OpenGL backend (an OpenGL 4.5 core EGL context drawing into an X11 window, under Wayland through Xwayland; without an X11 display the device does not start and the next one of the list is tried; the vendored backend changes are in `vendor/wgpu-sampler/PATCHES.md`), Vulkan by the Vulkan backend, and native plugins on OpenGL Core get a context in the renderer's share group, so a texture's native pointer is the renderer's own OpenGL texture, or a view of it where Unity's device has it with another target or in another format (the native rendering plugins row), of every shape (tests/projects/nativegfx `shape.*`: a cube map, a 2D array, a 3D texture, a cube map array, a multisampled render texture's resolved image; the native rendering plugins row). Nothing crosses between Vulkan and OpenGL any more: when Vulkan drew the OpenGL Core device, the renderer's images were imported into the plugins' OpenGL context, and handing their layouts over with semaphores was a follow-up; with a backend of its own for each device there is no such import, no code of it is left (no memory objects, no semaphores), and the follow-up is closed, not open. On OpenGL Core a graphics program samples a texture through the texture's own sampler state whatever sampler it names, an inline one or another texture's, in either stage, its comparisons too (tests/projects/samplerstates, 73/97 (24 declared residuals) with the residuals of the sampler states row; the Vulkan device samples through the sampler a program names, as the Vulkan Editor does, 73/97 (24 declared residuals)); compute kernels keep the states they name (tests/projects/renderapi computeSamplers). OpenGL samples through combined texture-and-sampler units, so where a program still samples one texture through several samplers (kernels, the other devices) it gets a texture variable for each, bound the same texture. A texture made with `CreateExternalTexture` from a plugin's own OpenGL texture is sampled in that texture's own format whatever format and color space the external texture declares, through its mip levels when it declares a mip chain (nativegfx `ext.*`). The anisotropic mip transition is each backend's own, as each Editor has it (tests/projects/samplerbias on both devices; the sampler states row). Measured on the NVIDIA driver only (the suite's OpenGL Core references run on it; other drivers may lack signed normalized render targets or sRGB framebuffers, which are asked of the driver or checked by `crates/unbound-app/src/render/gl_backend_tests.rs`); macOS draws every device with Metal and has no OpenGL plugin context. Open: the Vulkan Editor's GPU recorder and foveation answers (Unbound has neither); Metal. |

Its trees and details, collected with the terrain renderer, take the same mask (Unbound's choice, unmeasured; a renderer list filtering the terrain out is not measured either). Open: URP Terrain Lit shading residual in `terrain_light_layers` (mean 0.57, 689 pixels over 8: the whole Layer 1 terrain is darker and less blue away from the saturated area by the light, ambient or environment reflection-like; the Default terrain is saturated white in both, so it cannot show the same). |
| URP forward visible light order and per-object light choice beyond the per-object limit | `Graphics/Srp/SrpContext.cs` (`Culling.CollectVisibleLights`), `Graphics/LightImportance.cs`, `Graphics/FrameBuilder.cs` (`PerObjectLights`), `Graphics/ForwardLights.cs` | M | done (Q282; tests/projects/urp light-order.jsonl 40/40, `paths-opengl/many_lights_*` exact). The Manual documents only that Forward has a per-object limit (the URP asset's Per Object Limit, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/urp/rendering/forward-rendering-paths.html); the order is measured: an object's lights rank by the luminance of the colour as set times the intensity, local ones divided by 1 + 25 d² / range² from the bounds' centre, a shadowed directional light times 16, Important +1000, Not Important −1000, ties in visible order; `unity_LightData.y` counts all of them, `.z` is 0 without a main light or when its culling mask leaves out the layer; spot lights reach what their pyramid touches. Visible lights: intensity ≥ 0.001, culling mask sharing a layer with the camera's; local ones spot lights first, then point lights, each nearest the culling origin first; with maximumVisibleLights ≥ 0 the nearest stay (0: none). The built-in forward path ranks its pixel/vertex/SH lights with the same rule, and the Auto light that fills the pixel lights keeps (its importance − the next one's) / (the previous one's − the next one's) of its pixel pass, the rest going to a first vertex light (point, spot) or the harmonics (directional); the first vertex light gives up that share whichever light it is, a fourth vertex light is dropped, and the last vertex light fades the same way (tests/projects/builtinrp `light_rank_directional`, `light_rank_color` in a linear project, `light_rank_shadowed_sun`, `light_rank_directional_fill`, `light_rank_vertex`: means 0.04, 0.01, 0.02, 0.00, 1.06; tests/projects/lighting `five_points` 4.42 → 0.29). The Manual (built-in forward rendering) documents Important lights as always per-pixel and Auto ones per-pixel up to the Pixel Light Count; the ranking and fades are measured. The colour is taken as set (urp `color_temperature`, builtinrp `light_rank_color`). Not measured: a rotated renderer's oriented bounds against a point light's range (world bounds here), the spot pyramid's corners against a round cone, the order without a maximum (maximumVisibleLights −1: enabling order here), the directional lights' order among themselves (not sorted by Unity; brightest first here), ties at equal distance. |
| Sampler states of graphics programs: inline samplers (`sampler_PointClamp`, ...), other textures' samplers, comparisons, by device | `crates/unbound-shaders/src/samplers.rs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor.rs` | S | done (Q171, Q307, Q352) for Unity 6000.0.71f1 on the Editor's OpenGL Core and Vulkan devices, measured on the NVIDIA driver only (both Editors and Unbound ran on it; other drivers are not measured). The 6000.0 Manual (Using sampler states, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/SL-SamplerStates.html): "Inline samplers don't work if you target the OpenGL ES or OpenGL Core graphics APIs"; measured: each texture samples through its own state (tests/projects/urp `inline_sampler`: a Point/Repeat texture through `probe_linear_clamp_sampler` and a Bilinear/Clamp one through `probe_point_mirror_sampler`, outside 0..1; `upscale_point` equals `upscale_linear`). Graphics programs' samples through an inline sampler (named like no texture's, spelling a state) are rewritten in its SPIR-V to the texture's own sampler (`sampler<texture>`, declared when missing), in vertex and fragment stages. Compute kernels preserve predefined sampler states (Q274; `tests/projects/renderapi` `computeSamplers.*`). Kept: integer textures (read unfiltered); comparisons on the other devices keep the comparison sampler they name. Results: `upscale_point`, `postfx_film_grain`, `ssao_downsample` and `ssao_samples_high` became exact; the motion blur gathers got closer (`motion_blur_camera` 2926 to 1767 pixels over 8, `_high` 1093 to 249, `_objects` 268 to 110). A texture sampled through another texture's sampler is sampled through its own state on OpenGL Core too (Q307, tests/projects/samplerstates; graphics devices row), and so are its comparisons, whatever comparison sampler they name (Q352, samplerstates `CmpTwo.map`). A texture sampled with and without comparisons is two texture slots of one property, the depth texture with a comparison sampler and the texture as its other samples read it, each with the texture's own sampler of its kind (Q352, samplerstates `CmpOwn`, `CmpInline`, `CmpOther` and their `.map`/`.mapLinear` rows; 73/97 (24 declared residuals) records on OpenGL Core, NVIDIA). Fragment outputs go where their semantics say, by the DX9 names HLSLSupport.cginc gives them too: a CG program's `SV_Depth` is the depth it stores (it was a second color before; samplerstates `Fill`, `DepthOnly.depth`); into a target without a depth buffer such a program draws its colors alone, and one that writes only the depth leaves the colors as they were (`Fill.color`, `DepthOnly.color`). Residuals (samplerstates `residuals.json`): the Editor's comparison of a depth texture whose own state does not compare (a `RenderTextureFormat.Depth` target; a `Shadowmap`'s does) reads the stored depth through the texture's filter, where Unbound compares (10 rows, measured on NVIDIA only; undocumented, not modelled: a comparison cannot read the depth through a comparison sampler here); a 16-bit depth buffer is stored as 16 bits without stencil, as the Editor's (`DepthChannels.split.raw`, `.map.raw` exact; Q370), but a linear filter of it reads 0.400392 where the Editor reads 26240/65535 (suspected: Unbound filters a float copy of the depth) (`DepthChannels.splitLinear.raw`, `.mapLinear.raw`; the other depth-target rows are recorded in twentieths, which hides depth bits). A stencil test drawn into a 16-bit depth buffer passes everywhere (no stencil buffer, as documented for `RenderTexture.depth`), and a D16_UNorm_S8_UInt target keeps a stencil buffer and reads back as D24_UNorm_S8_UInt on both devices (samplerstates `Stencil.*`, recorded in the Editor; Unbound converts a format the device cannot render when it is set, the Editor measured after Create; a `.renderTexture` asset converts unless `m_EnableCompatibleFormat` is 0, and one with the fallback off and a 16-bit format with stencil is stored with 24 bits and stencil, the Editor's result for it not measured). Not supported where the device is drawn through OpenGL itself (OpenGL Core on Linux): a graphics program that compares a texture and also reads it with `Load` (OpenGL's shading language cannot load from a depth texture: the program is refused and draws with the error shader; the other devices draw it. Follow-ups: read the fetch from the plain slot when the program has one; compute kernels follow the same rules since Q370 (samplerstates `Compute.*`, both devices): a kernel's compared or depth-read texture reads the target's depth buffer, a far-plane default without one (a 2D-array or cube-array slot without a depth texture of its shape skips the dispatch with a warning; the Editor's result there not measured), its comparison sampler compares less or equal through the texture's state (mirrored on Vulkan's reversed depth), and on the OpenGL device a kernel that compares a texture and loads it is refused with an error (the Editor runs it, 4 rows); recorded differences: on Vulkan a comparison of a `Depth` target reads the stored depth in the Editor (4 rows, the mechanism above), on OpenGL Core the Editor's kernel comparisons of three of the four targets follow no one rule (3 rows, unexplained, suspected texture state left by earlier draws)). On the Vulkan device a graphics program samples through the sampler it names (Q352; samplerstates `--device vulkan`, 73/97 (24 declared residuals) records against the Vulkan Editor, NVIDIA): an inline sampler is the state its name spells (`Inline`, `CmpInline.*`, `DepthPlain.*`): one filter word, wrap words for the whole sampler or for the axes their `U`/`V`/`W` letters name (also along a volume's W), an anisotropic level of 2, 4, 8 or 16 (`InlineAxes`, `InlineV`, `InlineWords`, `InlineVolume`, `InlineAniso`: `ClampUV` names U alone, a whole-sampler `Repeat` wins over `MirrorOnce`, the axes a `MirrorOnceU` name leaves out mirror), and a wrongly spelt one (two filters, `Clamp` with `Repeat` or `Mirror`, another anisotropic level) is the Editor's error, word for word (`Bad*`; OpenGL Core, which does not read the names, draws those programs through the textures' own states); in a compute kernel a wrongly spelt name is logged and samples as the default (unmeasured there); another texture's sampler that texture's state, an inline `...Compare` sampler compares greater-or-equal on reversed depth, and a program with a sampler of a texture it does not use is refused in the Editor's words and draws with the error shader (`Shapes`: "Unrecognized sampler ... does not match any texture and is not a recognized inline name"; a `Load` of the texture or its use in the other stage counts: `UsedLoad`, `UsedVertex`); the rewrite to a texture's own sampler is OpenGL Core's alone (`GraphicsDevice::samples_through_texture_state`; Metal follows the rule of the devices that name their samplers, unmeasured). Residuals on Vulkan (`residuals-vulkan.json`): the same two mechanisms, a comparison through the own sampler of a `Depth` target reading the stored depth in the Editor (12 rows) and a linear filter of a 16-bit depth buffer (2 raw rows), and `MirrorOnce`, inline as for a texture's own wrap mode, sampled as `Mirror` (`InlineMirrorOnce`; the MirrorOnce row). tests/projects/samplerbias is recorded on the Vulkan Editor too (`Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/vulkan`, every profile, two identical captures each): with anisotropic filtering the Vulkan Editor's mip transition starts at a whole level where OpenGL Core's is centred on a half, as each backend has it natively, so the correction that centred it when Vulkan drew the OpenGL Core device is removed (all 8 profiles match on both devices: 94834/94834 observations in the default one) |

| Script float arithmetic in single precision (wider intermediates in measured Editor expressions) | `managed/Unbound.Host/WideFloats.cs`, `WideFloats.Expressions.cs`, `HotReload.cs`, `Runtime/Compat.cs` (`ScriptFloatsWide`) | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 script, package and precompiled plugin assemblies (tests/projects/floatrounding: Release 172/226 and Debug 181/226 values, 0 failures, the listed residuals below; the Linux x86_64 and macOS arm64 Editors record identical values in both modes, two captures each). In the Editor a float on the evaluation stack is a double: chains, comparisons, conversions (`(int)`, `(double)`, `Math.Sqrt`) and values left on the stack across calls and joins keep it, and it is rounded to float where the IL stores it (locals, arguments, fields, array elements, indirect stores, kept boxes, closure/iterator/async fields, every call argument and return value, also of inlined, generic, interface and delegate calls, and `conv.r4`); `box` straight into `unbox.any` keeps it. Debug and Release differ only where the compiled IL keeps a local. Decision: a load-time IL rewrite, because it covers source-compiled and precompiled assemblies alike and no Roslyn option or CoreCLR knob reproduces the rule (Q402 controls). Unbound converts to double the float loads of each expression whose result double evaluation can change (an operation on another operation's result, or a result that is compared, converted or used other than by rounding it to float); one operation rounded straight away gives the same float and stays as it is; the runtime rounds stores and arguments ([ECMA-335](https://ecma-international.org/publications-and-standards/standards/ecma-335/) III.1.1.1, III.1.6). Cost, macOS arm64 benchmark: a particle-style struct loop 1.5x, a scalar polynomial loop 1.2x (converting every float load would cost 3.3x by defeating inlining). Scope: `[BurstCompile]` methods and the `Execute` of `[BurstCompile]` job structs keep float arithmetic (the other methods of such types, and the methods Burst-compiled code calls, are converted; Burst in the Editor is unmeasured); a hot-reload edit is patched in place unless an edited method needs the conversions, which restarts the session (deltas are not converted); reflection on a converted method body shows the conversions (69 `il/` residuals). Open: Unity's managed math (Mathf, Vector3, Quaternion, Matrix4x4) is engine code evaluated in float apart from the measured Quaternion Dot and Normalize (30 `api/`/`fuzz/` residuals, queued); other releases keep CoreCLR's float evaluation, unmeasured. The [C# specification 8.3.7](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/language-specification/types#837-floating-point-types) permits these wider intermediates. URP's `(int)(pixelWidth * renderScale)` (tests/projects/urp upscale_auto_fractional) is package code under the same rule and is re-checked by the Linux GPU suite; tests/projects/particletriggerboundary keeps its explicit double arithmetic, which equals the rule |

Open: no probe of our own compares probe volume lighting with the Editor, because the Editor cannot bake probe volumes on the measured machine (its light transport fails with "RadeonRays intersection API object is invalid" with the CPU and GPU lightmappers, on OpenGL Core and Vulkan); the sample's frames are compared by eye only; `IsMainAssetAtPathLoaded` is measured for ScriptableObject assets only (an unloaded texture, clip or material Unbound reloads in place keeps answering true; a prefab only instanced by a scene and ProjectSettings assets answer false: unmeasured) |
