| Buffers held in structs (URP's Adaptive Probe Volume `APVResources`) and more than 16 textures in a stage (Q418) | `crates/unbound-shaders/src/buffer_members.rs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/compiled.rs` (`texture_bindings_refused`), `vendor/wgpu-sampler` (`gl-device.patch`) | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 on the OpenGL Core device (tests/projects/shaderresources, 11/11, measured on the NVIDIA driver only): every URP Lit variant with `PROBE_VOLUMES_L1` failed glslang (a `StructuredBuffer` member of a struct) and drew with the error shader. A buffer member that every assignment in the program gives the same global buffer is that buffer (HLSL resolves a resource held in a struct statically); a member given several globals or anything else is left for glslang to report. The OpenGL backend binds the context's texture units per stage (32 on the measured driver) where it bound 16: URP Lit with probe volumes binds 21 (the pipeline was refused and the renderer stopped). A program over the device's sampled textures or samplers per stage now draws with the error shader and a warning instead. Unity's own compiler refuses more than 16 sampler states in a stage; Unbound binds a sampler per texture variable, so on a device with 16 a program of more than 15 textures is refused (the renderer's main texture takes one unit) |
| Shader targets and GPU feature requirements per variant: `#pragma target` and `#pragma require` with and without keywords (Q394) | `crates/unbound-shaders/src/targets.rs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor.rs` (instancing keyword) | S | done (Q394, tests/projects/shadertargets on OpenGL Core and Vulkan, 110/110 each, measured on the NVIDIA driver only): a line with keywords applies to the variants with any of them (the Unity 6000.0 Manual, Targeting shader models and GPU features in HLSL); Unbound read the last `#pragma target` for every variant (URP Lit's `#pragma target 4.5 DOTS_INSTANCING_ON` made all its variants 4.5). Measured: a variant's target is the highest its applying lines give, in any order (2.5 without a plain target); plain 4.0 compiles as 3.5 and plain 4.6 and 5.0 as 4.5 in a program without geometry or tessellation stages, a keyed line keeps its target; required features raise the target on their own (`mrt4` 3.0, `2darray` and `instancing` 3.5, `geometry` 4.0 (keyed), `compute` and `msaatex` 4.5, `tessellation` 4.6 (keyed), `compute` with `tessellation` 5.0 (keyed); `integers` and `cubearray` nothing); `SHADOWS_SCREEN`, `SHADOWS_SOFT`, `DIRLIGHTMAP_COMBINED`, `DYNAMICLIGHTMAP_ON` make a variant 3.0 and `PROCEDURAL_INSTANCING_ON` 4.5; a variant requiring sparse textures draws with the error shader on both devices (though `SystemInfo.supportsSparseTextures` answers true; measured in a shader without a Fallback or a further SubShader); the `SHADER_AVAILABLE_*` defines are the variant's features and the device's own (3.5's on OpenGL Core, 4.5's on Vulkan, which the earlier per-target table left out), never the stages', cube arrays only through `#pragma require cubearray` (the earlier table, after the Manual's pragma target reference, gave them to 4.6), and `SHADER_TARGET_AVAILABLE` is defined; `es3.0`, `es3.1`, `gl4.1` targets apply nothing on these devices (the Manual's reference describes `gl4.1` as 4.0 with cube arrays and tessellation); a material's `INSTANCING_ON` selects no variant (the draw decides). A target value or feature name Unity does not know is left out of every variant with a warning, once per shader file and name, naming the file (Q398; what the Editor reports for such lines is unmeasured). Unmeasured: the instancing variant's target (taken as 3.5, the instancing feature's), a material's `INSTANCING_ON` through a renderer's draw (only `Graphics.Blit` recorded) and a global one, compute kernels (now 5.0's features without the stages', so `SHADER_AVAILABLE_TESSHW` is no longer defined for them, and `SHADER_TARGET_AVAILABLE` 45), the other features whose variants may be refused (taken as compiled), plain (unkeyed) requires of stage features, the stereo and device capability keywords (Unbound enables none), Metal (taken as OpenGL Core's features) |
| Indirect draws' `startInstance` and instancing cost | `Graphics/GraphicsDraws.cs`, `IndirectDraws.cs`, `CommandStream.cs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor.rs` | W | done: every indirect path (DrawMeshInstancedIndirect, DrawProceduralIndirect with and without indices, their CommandBuffer forms, RenderMeshIndirect, RenderPrimitivesIndirect, RenderPrimitivesIndexedIndirect) draws `SV_InstanceID` from 0 whatever the start instance, as the OpenGLCore Editor does (tests/projects/cmdbuffers `render.indexed.startinstance.*`); the arguments go through one pooled buffer without ending the render pass. Pool growth and reuse across 257 draws and a fragment-written arguments buffer are covered by `cmdbuffers indirect.pool.*`. Instanced calls copy their property block once and stream each array once per hand-over (render protocol 12) |

#### Rendering rules measured on one release

These answers of `Runtime/Compat.cs` give the measured behaviour to exactly 6000.0.71f1
(`Compat.RenderingMeasured`). Every other release, including other 6000.0 patches, takes the
unmeasured answer in the last column until a probe records it.

| Rule (`Compat.`) | Measured by (6000.0.71f1) | Other releases (unmeasured) |
|---|---|---|
| `RenderingMeasured`: world-space skinning retains its renderer's winding separately from the submitted model | samplerstates `RendererFaces.skinnedRenderer*`, `RendererFaces.commandSkinned*`, NVIDIA OpenGL Core/Vulkan | only the submitted model determinant is corrected |
| `ImmediateMeshKeepsWinding`: DrawMeshNow and CommandBuffer.DrawMesh preserve submitted winding | samplerstates `StencilFaces.*`, `RendererFaces.*` on OpenGL Core and Vulkan, NVIDIA only | determinant correction as for renderers |
| `MeshInstanceBatchLimits`: mesh-instancing IDs restart at 511/1023 transforms or 64 KiB of properties | cmdbuffers `graphics.meshinstances.*.batch.*` | sequential instance IDs, with a once-only warning |
| `GraphicsFenceFrames`: a CPU fence reports passed two frames after it ran | cmdbuffers `fence.*` | passed as soon as the GPU has passed it |
| `ZeroIndexedInstancesDrawOne`: `RenderPrimitivesIndexed` with 0 instances draws one | cmdbuffers `render.indexed.instances.zero` | draws none |
| `SkyAmbientWithoutLightColor`: the ambient projection of the sky draws it with `_LightColor0` zero (any sky material; only procedural skies with every disk mode were measured, so leaving out the colour rather than only the disk is a hypothesis until a sky tinted by `_LightColor0` is captured) | indirectspec, Q105 ambient controls | the sky projected as drawn, sun disk included |
| `SingleSubpassClearFillsOutputs`: a subpass with several color outputs of which exactly one clears clears them all to its color | urp `subpass_mrt`, `subpass_mrt_cleared`, `subpass_mrt_mixed` | each clearing output to its own color, the others kept |
| `RenderingMeasured`: texture readback conversion fills missing color components with 1, for byte, normalized, half and float sources | renderapi `volumeReadback.*`, 2D and 3D targets | R16/RG32 normalized sources fill with 1; other sources keep sampler padding |
| `ShaderKeywordsMeasured`: the built-in keyword registry and types | shaderkeywords | every name a global keyword, with a warning |
| `RenderingMeasured`: automatic render-target mips become visible when a subsequent draw prepares changed framebuffer attachments; public GenerateMips rejects automatic targets | renderapi `volumeMore.*`, `volumeAuto.*`, 2D and volume controls | automatic mips update after each render pass; GenerateMips is accepted |

A Don't Clear camera's intermediate that does not take the screen's depth starts with cleared
depth (measured for partial screen cameras and explicit full-rect HDR-to-byte targets,
cameraexplicitdepth). Explicit targets with image effects follow the same rule unmeasured.

in ROADMAP; NavMesh queries/builds/agents/avoidance; terrain heights, collider, details.

| `Simulate` without restart on a stopped system | `Particles/ParticleSystem.cs` (`StopSelf`, `AdvanceStoppedClock`), `ParticleSystem.Simulation.cs` (`Advance`, `AdvanceEmissionStep`, `CollectBursts`) | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/particleemission, 17171/17171 records; `longCall/false/*` and the added `stoppedSimulate/*`, `stoppedResume/*`, `stoppedLongStep/*`, `disabledBurst/*`, `mixedRemainder/*` and `stoppedChild/*` controls): `Stop` with either behaviour stops emission whatever the system did before, also one that never played or whose GameObject was inactive, and only a playback start clears it, so a non-restarting `Simulate` afterwards advances the clock without emitting and leaves the system paused; `Play` then resumes at that time. A fresh system that was never stopped emits. A stopped system keeps what is left of its start delay (`StopEmittingAndClear` no longer drops it; one that never played takes its start delay first): a step no longer than the delay leaves the clock where it is, a longer one advances it by the excess. Only an emitting system whose advance starts at playback time zero counts its delay down (a fresh playback; its step that ends the delay spreads its rate births over the rest of that step); one resumed at a later time with delay left keeps it for good, advancing by the excess of each step (not at all with steps no longer than the delay) and spreading births over the whole step. A never-played Birth sub-emitter stopped with its parent stays at time zero. A burst cycle whose time passes while the system does not emit (stopped, or its emission module off) does not fire when emission resumes in that loop; later cycles do. Fixed-step and variable-step calls share one remainder: what a fixed-step `Simulate` leaves below one step, and the sub-microsecond residual of a variable-step call, join the next advance of either kind until a new playback (a variable-step call keeps the equal steps of its own time and steps the carried time last); with it a resumed rate counts the particle due just after a loop end. A random start delay of a system that never played is unmeasured |
| Sub-emitter rotation inheritance and particle storage | `Particles/ParticleSystem.cs` (`RefreshParticleStorage`, `has3DParticleRotations`, `hasNonUniformParticleSizes`), `ParticleSystem.Simulation.cs` (sub-emitter emission, `CurrentSize3D`, `InitParticle`), `ParticleTypes.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/particleseedaxis, 110/110 records; the 48 values of `manual_*_15_*` and the added `inherit_*`, `storage_*` and `rotation_emit` controls): `InheritRotation` adds the parent particle's z rotation to every child and its x and y rotations only to a child whose own start rotation is 3D. What a system's particles keep beyond a z rotation and a uniform size (3D rotations: a 3D start rotation, shape `alignToDirection`, separate-axes rotation over lifetime or by speed; non-uniform sizes: a 3D start size, separate-axes size modules; rotation speeds: either rotation module) is decided from the modules when the system updates (also by a `Simulate` call shorter than a step), emits from a script or takes `SetParticles`, not when a module changes, and not by a script's `TriggerSubEmitter` into it (a parent's Birth, Death, Collision or Trigger emission takes the storage the child's modules ask for: Unbound's choice, unmeasured). `has3DParticleRotations` and `hasNonUniformParticleSizes` report it. A child that never updated since a module asked for more therefore drops its own and the inherited x and y rotations, takes its x size on all axes, and keeps no rotation speed; a parent with uniform-size storage passes its x size. Particles a script supplies through the 3D setters (`SetParticles`, `Emit` with `EmitParams`) keep 3D storage until a playback restarts without particles. A particle emitted from a script has its rotation modules' speed at once. Unmeasured: whether a particle's speed-based rotation (`rotationBySpeed`) at birth uses its start velocity, and how `SetTriggerParticles` with 3D setters affects the storage (it does not change it) |

| `NavMeshObstacle` avoidance and carving | `AI/NavMeshObstacle.cs` (ObstacleSystem), `AI/NavMeshCarving.cs`, `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (crowd feed, rebuild) | S | carving done for measured cases (tests/projects/navmesh, `run.py unbound`: 227/227 `carve.*` and per-frame `holes` values): box holes are the outline grown by the agent radius with sharp corners (turned box included), with Unity's NavMesh vertices and heights around them; a change is decided after Update (PreLateUpdate) and cut in the next frame's PreUpdate (made in Update of frame N: cut from N + 1; in LateUpdate: from N + 2), a new obstacle is carved at once, carveOnlyStationary keeps the hole for a 0.05 m move, removes it for a 1.25 m move and cuts it again after more than 0.5 s, and turning carving off removes it; agents replan in the frame of the cut. How a hole splits the polygons matches Unity's (tests/projects/navpath `carve.sides`, `carve.mud`: every `polyX.*` piece and the routes round them; navcost `fans.carvebox`): the polygons a hole reaches are taken in groups connected inside the tile, each group's hole is the convex hull of what the hole covers of it, each polygon is cut by the hull's edges into triangles (an edge inside the polygon with the polygon's corner farthest outside it, fans from the hull's corners over the corners between) merged in order into convex pieces of at most six vertices; points within a 64th of a voxel are one vertex, and new vertices take the height of the last cut piece's source detail surface. Measured too (navpath `carve.rules`, 549 of 551 values): a capsule's hole is an octagon round its axis at its radius over cos 22.5 degrees (upright, turned, lying), outlines are grown with one point at corners turning up to about 90 degrees and cut across at sharper ones, an obstacle of no width or depth cuts nothing, holes are cut in order of their centres (x, then z, then y; the larger first round one centre), not in the order the obstacles were made, an obstacle carves the polygons whose detail heights its height span reaches, extended down by the agent height (a top 0.3 m under the floor and a bottom 2.1 m over it do not carve, 1.9 m does), and a turn counts as movement by its angle times the lengths of the obstacle's scale and world extents, a scaling by the scale change times the extents' length (5.7 degrees and 8 % of a 1 m box at threshold 0.1 do, 3 degrees and 4 % do not; 3 degrees of a 2 m box and of a 0.5 m box at scale 2 do, and 4 % of a 2 m box; the hole comes back in the 27th frame). Setting size, centre, radius, height or shape (even to the same value; shape only when it changes) cuts the hole again at the next decision as it is then, and that decision does not count a move (a stationary-only obstacle moving 5 cm a frame while its size is set every frame is cut every frame); a stationary-only obstacle that keeps creeping after a move (5 mm a frame) stays uncut, as it is still only once it stays within a tenth of the threshold of where it was last seen moving. Residual (listed, not gated: navpath `carve.rules` poly.p47, poly.p51): where an exact tie or the order of a cut piece's vertices decides how its remainder is split, the result follows the vertex the built tile polygon starts at, and Unity's floor tiles start at another corner than Unbound's in some tiles (measured: rotating the base polygon of the tiles at negative z by two vertices reproduces Unity's split there); the area cut is the same. Unbound's choice, unmeasured: a cut obstacle not also avoided. A carving change cuts again only the tiles the changed holes reach and replaces them in place; other polygon references are kept. Unbound's choice (unmeasured, as was the earlier replan of every agent): only agents whose corridor, link or polygon lay in a replaced tile rejoin and replan (managed NavMeshCarvingTests.ACutReplacesOnlyTheTilesTheHoleReachesAndTheirAgentsPaths), so an agent whose corridor avoids a tile where a hole is removed keeps its longer way, as Unity's does (navpath `carve.shortcut`: every value matches since agents shorten their path while walking, the walking-agents row). The blocked agent's route past the hole matches Unity's since the path-cost row (Q37); its remaining differences are the agents' movement (as the other agents'). Avoidance: partial, measured against Unity 6000.0.71f1 (navpath `replan.none` retains 17/578 differing values: coordinate errors of 1.4 to 4.7 cm, position distance up to 5.7 cm; the separate avoidance probe retains four failing groups, 34 configurations): enabled obstacles are avoidance obstacles in the agents' crowds (box/capsule as circles, scripted velocity, height span; Unbound's choices, unmeasured). A box is at most 16 circles, each at most sqrt(2) half-widths (longer boxes warn once); only circles that can be a walking agent's neighbour are fed, and an agent whose 32 neighbour candidates still overflow with obstacles among them warns once; Managed validation found the six-circle 5.5 m wall can stall an agent outside the box; no Unity capture establishes parity for that case. |
| NavMesh path search picks another route than Unity where Detour's edge-midpoint A* costs disagree with Unity's | `AI/DotRecast/DotRecast.Detour/DtNavMeshQuery.cs` (UpdateSlicedFindPath, PlaceNode, PolyHeuristic), `AI/NavMesh.cs` (PathSearch) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost: every `strip.*`, `bake.*` and `carve.*` route sweep, 31 sweeps over area costs and path ends on baked and carved polygons; navmesh `paths.expensiveWater` and the blocked agent's route corners; managed NavMeshPathCostTests on the probe's strip polygons). Documented (AI Navigation 2.0 manual, Areas and Costs): cost is distance times the area cost of the polygon, nodes are placed on polygons, the result is not always the shortest path. Measured: a polygon's node is placed once, when the search first reaches the polygon, at the portal point nearest the node it is reached from (in 3D, kept 5% of the portal from its ends); later cheaper ways change its cost and parent, not its place; the heuristic of a polygon is 0.999 times the distance from the end to the polygon; `FindPath` and the sliced search are one search. Straight paths (tests/projects/navpath `cost.*`, `hole.*`): Unity's funnel tests the portal ends as offsets from the apex with components within 1e-4 counted as zero, makes a corner only where a portal lies strictly beyond a side (a path through a portal end exactly has none), tests both sides before narrowing either, and makes off-mesh connection ends corners where the path crosses onto or off a connection, also in line; a start within 2 mm of the first portal begins past it. Residuals: one carved piece of navcost `fans.carvebar` starts its triangle fan at another vertex than Unity's (listed, not gated: same shape; two polygon corners lie equally far outside the hole's edge and Unity's hull corners differ from Unbound's in the last float bit, so each picks the other corner); a line grazing a mesh vertex that the builders place about 2e-6 apart, on either side of the line, keeps its corner in Unity (navpath `two.p1`, listed); the straight path still drops a corner the path passes within about 1e-4 of slightly later than Unity in 8 navcost values (Known insignificant residuals). The runtime raster-bounds correction (Q376) resolves the two initial `replan.topofar` corner counts over a floor cut by thin water strips: `a.given` and `mask.given` are now 7 in both engines, with all 1687 values matching the unchanged reference. Measured (navpath `carve.shortcut`, matching): Unity's agent keeps its way round when a carved gap opens in tiles its path does not cross. |

| Runtime links (`NavMesh.AddLink`) whose end lies in another NavMesh tile than their start never connect (a runtime-built NavMesh is tiled from the world origin, so any link across a tile edge such as x = 0 or z = 0) | `AI/NavWorld.cs` (AddLinks) | S | partial, measured against Unity 6000.0.71f1 (Q361 ran all six navigation probes and 233 managed navigation tests): a link's end is classified against the tile its start lies over, as DotRecast's own tile builder classifies baked connections, so an end over that tile or one of its eight neighbours lands there (both ways when bidirectional); a start on a tile edge is added to the tiles on both sides, each copy classified against its own tile. Each tile also reserves three link slots per link end over it or within the link snap radius of it, for the ways back of the links that land in it: more than that builder's two for baked connection ends, because a start on a tile corner is added to all four tiles round it and up to three of those copies land in the same tile besides its own. Since the placed NavMesh data row: an end lands in the tiles it lies over (in each NavMesh's own frame), of any NavMesh and however far from its start (navpath `place.links`: a 9 m link and a 2 m wide link onto a copy moved, and moved and turned 30 degrees, over 5.33 m tiles); an end just past the NavMesh within the snap radius of its polygons lands on them. Open multilayer ownership and capacity scope is now measured by the Q385 navpath layers diagnostic, documented below. The reservation still assumes polygons lie inside their tile bounds up to float rounding and ground links stay within the builder's slot budget; neither assumption establishes a safe general capacity bound. The existing route and landing controls were run in Q361; their remaining query differences are listed below. Measured (Unity 6000.0.71f1, tests/projects/navlinks `crosstile`): a NavMeshLink added at run time across a baked NavMesh's tile edge (x = 42.67) connects and is crossed both ways, as in Unbound. Link ends (Unity 6000.0.71f1, tests/projects/navpath `link.snap`, navlinks `snap`, `snapnear`, `snapfar`): an end attaches to the nearest polygon within the agent type's radius across (2 m for a 2 m type) and its climb in height, every link alike; a partial path ends on the ground, never on a link whose far end is not attached. Wide links (`width` > 0; navpath `link.wide`, `link.wideoff`, navlinks `wide`): each end is a segment of the link's width along its up crossed with its direction, attached to every polygon it crosses and to no other (no snap across: an end 0.2 m off the NavMesh does not attach), the part over each polygon kept with the link; paths cross straight at their lateral offset, held to the parts over the NavMesh; an agent's nextOffMeshLinkData is reported from its path's crossing point, and it crosses straight across from where it reaches the link; a tile's links grow when a wide link needs more than it reserved (an end over more than 32 polygons of a tile is cut short, with a warning). A path leaving a wide link beyond its width turns at the far end of that end (`link.wide` a3: Unity's funnel swaps a portal seen reversed but keeps its points); crossings are where the path's line comes nearest the link's end; a wide link's ends attach in every tile they reach (`link.widetile`, across the tile edge at z = 0; a link starting on a tile edge is in both tiles, as point links, so its ends are attached twice: two equal connections). A ground portal seen reversed from the funnel's apex (behind it, in a U-turn) is unmeasured. Listed, not gated: `link.wide` retains two values, `gb1.2.cur.start.z` 5.984523 in the Editor versus 5.817135 in Unbound (16.7 cm), and `cur.at.z` 5.86303425 versus 5.817135 (4.6 cm). Correcting which landing polygon traversal uses does not resolve this approach discrepancy; see the Q342 residual row. |

Measured: a NavMesh of 4 x 4 tiles moved off the tile grid, turned 30 and 90 degrees, answers SamplePosition on every tile, rays across tile edges and round walls and paths across tiles as where it was built (rays, nearest polygons and tile lookups in its own frame; straight-path corners in line may drop differently in world axes, as Unity's do); NavMeshes built and added separately do not connect, even where their tiles meet at a tile edge (a path from one to the other is partial); a runtime build ends within one voxel past its bounds (the bounds rounded out to whole voxels, clipped to each tile). Each data instance is a surface with its own tile grid: queries turn their box (clipped to the surface's bounds) into the surface's frame and visit surfaces in the order they were added, tiles along x, then z. Agents walking round a wall's end take the shorter way a few seconds later, as Unity's (`place.moved` walk, every pose; the walking-agents row). Unmeasured: NavMesh data tilted out of the horizontal (queries rank by height along the world's up, agents walk in the world's horizontal plane); Links land on moved and turned NavMeshes (`place.links`); the route a path takes past a link's landing differs from Unity's in some cases (`place.links` m.p.ab, r30.p.ab, r30.w.ab: Unity's bends at a tile corner of the landing NavMesh; m.w.ba: which part of a wide end over two tiles it crosses), the same moved or turned, listed, not gated (the link rows). |
| `NavMesh.Raycast` stopped by the area mask reports mask 0 | `AI/NavMesh.cs` (Raycast), DotRecast `DtNavMeshQuery.Raycast` (`DtRaycastHit.blockedRef`) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost `raycast`, `raycast.agent`; navbaked `raycast`; managed NavMeshRaycastMaskTests): a ray stopped where the filter excludes the polygon beyond reports that polygon's area (also after crossing allowed areas, and starting inside the excluded area, where the ray starts on the nearest allowed polygon), one stopped at the NavMesh's border reports 0, a clear ray its end polygon's area; NavMeshAgent.Raycast the same with the agent's mask. A source with no allowed polygon within the query box (off the NavMesh, or on an area the mask excludes with no allowed polygon near) is no hit: false, position and distance infinite, mask 0 (tests/projects/navpath `ray.area`). Unmeasured: an excluded polygon beyond a tile border (found the same way). |
| NavMeshAgent writes its transform onto the NavMesh as soon as it is placed | `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (AgentSystem.SyncTransforms, Place, FollowTransform, OnAddedByScript, baseOffset, updatePosition), `Core/Component.cs`, `Runtime/Components.cs` | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost `place.*`, every frame from Awake to frame 14; managed NavMeshAgentPlacementTests): an agent enabled by its scene, by activation or by `enabled`, or warped, is on the NavMesh at once (nextPosition snapped, also in x and z beside the edge) while its transform stays where it was until the next frame's early AI update (PreUpdate), which writes it; AddComponent and setting baseOffset write it at once; a transform moved by script is followed by nextPosition at once and written back at the next early update (the update after the scripts leaves standing agents' transforms alone); an agent not enabled reports its transform as nextPosition. Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, NavMeshAgent.nextPosition): the transform matches the simulated position when Update is called (so every agent standing elsewhere than its transform, also after a script sets nextPosition or completes a link, is written at the early update), and turning updatePosition back on moves the transform to it: measured (tests/projects/navpath `sync.updatePosition`), at the next early AI update, not in the setter; a transform moved while updatePosition was off (in Update or LateUpdate, with or without a changed nextPosition) does not move the agent, one moved again after turning it on does, and turning it off takes in a script move made before. A transform moved by script with updatePosition on (in Update or LateUpdate) is written onto the NavMesh at the next early AI update. Agents are placed within 20 of their radii across and 15 up and down from the NavMesh, as SetDestination maps its target (navpath `snap.reach`, `snap.reachtype`); unmeasured: radii below 0.5, scaled transforms, and the same reach re-placing agents after a NavMesh change (carving included: an agent in a new hole is moved up to that reach away). Unmeasured: Instantiate of an active agent (treated as activation), Move (writes at once, as before), baseOffset set while the transform waits for the early update (written at once), nextPosition of an enabled agent off the NavMesh (its transform). Walking agents move in the early AI update before Update (see the agent update row); the agent whose destination is set at frame 7 drifts in z from frame 12 (avoidance near standing agents, navcost `walk.later`, listed, not gated, as the navmesh walkers). |
| NavMeshAgents move after the scripts' Update (PreLateUpdate), so Update sees them one step behind | `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (AgentSystem.Step, PlanAtOnce, Rotate, LeaveLink, velocity, isStopped, Warp, nextPosition, remainingDistance), `AI/DotRecast/DotRecast.Detour.Crowd/DtCrowd.cs` (Update), `DtCrowdAgent.cs` (NextVelocity) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost `loop.*`: 21 lanes at four points of 20 frames, 15 gated, and `walk.*`; navlinks failing values 98403 -> 83752, its warp lanes 1014 -> 812 and 1033 -> 830; managed NavMeshAgentLoopTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, NavMeshAgent.nextPosition): the transform matches the simulated position when Update is called; the player loop's AI systems (PreUpdate.AIUpdate, PreLateUpdate.AIUpdatePostScript) are listed by PlayerLoop.GetDefaultPlayerLoop, their work is not documented. Measured: the whole agent update runs once per frame in PreUpdate.AIUpdate: transforms moved by script are followed, pending paths found, the crowds steer, plan, move (by the plan of the update before), reach links from where the step took them and begin to cross them, transforms and rotations are written; AIUpdatePostScript changes no agent (a request pending in Update stays pending through LateUpdate). SetDestination gives the path in the call when it needs no search: the destination on the agent's own polygon, or, for an agent following a path, on the rest of that path (not behind it); else at the next AIUpdate (also for agents made that frame). The agent turns towards the velocity its next step takes (on an automatic link: along the current approach or crossing phase velocity, holding orientation when that phase is stationary; its kept plan while manual traversal waits) by (1 + that speed) times the angle left per second, at most angularSpeed, so it turns from the update that plans its first step. A velocity set by script is stepped at the next update and reported from then; isStopped slows the agent at its acceleration after the step already planned; Warp stops it at once; setting nextPosition writes the transform at once (Move as before); the update after CompleteOffMeshLink walks on; remainingDistance is infinite on a link. A stale path (navlinks `inactive`: SetDestination after its link was activated) waits for the next AIUpdate too, the agent reporting its current path meanwhile (see the destination row). Unbound's choices, unmeasured: the path given at once is the agent's corridor cut at the destination's polygon; a destination to the side of a walking agent off its path waits; one on the agent's polygon ahead of it while it walks is given at once. Applies to every release; only 6000.0.71f1 is measured. Turning control (navlinks `turning`: 1.5 m/s at 40°/s and 6 m/s at 400°/s): the rule holds (4 of 1604 rotation values off by under 2e-3). Residuals (navcost, listed): after a destination behind a walking agent, the turn while its velocity passes through zero ends 0.47 degrees off and stays so (`loop.redirect`, `loop.redirectOff`, rotation only, from frame 12; the slower velocity of the update that applies the path now matches, see the local avoidance row); a destination re-set to itself picks up a sideways desired velocity for three updates in Unity (`loop.redirectSame`, cause not found: the lane's own neighbours walk parallel to it); the turn after a velocity set by script is up to 0.13 degrees off (`loop.velocity`); see also the open rows below. |
| NavMeshAgent local avoidance: which neighbours and NavMesh edges an agent avoids, and how it samples | `AI/DotRecast/DotRecast.Detour.Crowd/DtCrowd.cs` (BuildProximityGrid, UpdateBoundary, GetNeighbours, PlanVelocity, AvoidanceShare), `DtObstacleAvoidanceQuery.cs`, `DtObstacleAvoidanceParams.cs`, `DtLocalBoundary.cs`, `DtProximityGrid.cs`, `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (SizeGrids, steeringTarget), `AI/NavMeshObstacle.cs` (Feed) | S | partial, with measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 mechanisms (tests/projects/avoidance: differing records 1060 -> 754, every set as good or better; navlinks differing values 46187 -> 27134, no lane worse, `fast` 183 -> 1 and `widetype` 148 -> 0; navcost `walk.later` 12 -> 0 and now gated, `loop.redirect*` 128/320 -> 36 (rotation only); navmesh differing scenarios 251 -> 216; managed NavMeshAgentAvoidanceTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0 NavMeshAgent: obstacleAvoidanceType, avoidancePriority, avoidancePredictionTime, desiredVelocity "including any potential contribution from avoidance", steeringTarget): agents avoid others and obstacles, more important ones fully. Measured (one rule for every lane): agents choose as neighbours the agents of a proximity grid (cells sized from every agent's diameter and speed within the prediction time, at least 1 m) with each neighbour inserted over its radius and the travel of its current velocity within the prediction time (Q385), around the square of their radius plus their full-speed reach within the prediction time, as or more important than them, heights meeting, not behind them along the way they last meant to go, reachable within the prediction time, the soonest 8; their NavMesh edges are the nearest 8 within their radius plus half their reach, none while the corner they last steered by is their path's end or a link within that reach; sampling keeps a desired velocity with nothing to avoid, else weighs the desired velocity first, then Detour's adaptive rings around 0.7 of it offset right, by 0.2 (desired), 0.1 (current) and 2/(0.1 + time to impact / prediction time), against each neighbour's velocity shared by responsibility (alone against a more important neighbour or one that does not avoid it in turn, half between equals) and against edges facing them (time doubled); a path given between updates shows in steeringTarget and the edge rule from the agent's next update (navlinks `incall`, `pendingold`). Agents without avoidance gather no neighbours (no agents, no obstacle circles) and are never pushed out of overlaps, although the AI Navigation 2.0 manual says for Quality None that collisions resolve (measured: navpath `replan.*`, agents without avoidance started at one point walk on together; `replan.none`, one walks through a NavMeshObstacle capsule); agents that avoid still gather them and avoid them alone (`replan.none`: of two started 0.3 m apart only the one with avoidance is pushed out). Unbound's choices, unmeasured: a stopped agent looks for neighbours and edges as far as its speed setting reaches; obstacle-circle footprints count in the measured prediction profile's grid size; box obstacles retain their existing circle approximation, unmeasured here; other releases retain agent-only sizing; agents of different agent types do not avoid each other (one crowd per agent type). Q385 prediction bounds reduce the avoidance probe from five failing groups/41 configurations to four groups/34 configurations: moving 5 -> 2, long runs 3 -> 0, speeds 18 -> 17; near 10 and overlap 5 remain. No newly failing record is introduced in any set. Baked-mesh agent records improve for climber 196 -> 3 and right 225 -> 1; 212 distinct frame scenarios remain, mainly walker steering and blocked corner timing. All 36 added prediction/finite-input scenarios match two identical GPU Editor captures, preserving every earlier reference row byte for byte. Nonfinite radius and velocity assignments log an error and retain the previous values; invalid or overflowing stored prediction inputs retain point insertion to keep the cell traversal bounded. `Compat.NavMeshPredictionBoundsMeasured` selects this mechanism for 6000.0.71f1; other releases retain the previous point insertion/input handling, unmeasured there. Q385 orientation and endpoint reporting subsequently reduce navlinks to 374 values, including crosstile 120; two now matches. Post steering remains 17 values. Navpath's post-avoidance residual is recorded below. The earlier mechanisms remain unmeasured on other releases; Q385 prediction and input validation apply only to the measured release. Q401: the reported steering target is the first corner found after the step (see the orientation, endpoint and stopping row); this takes the avoidance probe from 34 to 3 configurations (moving 2 -> 0, near 10 -> 2, overlap 5 -> 0, speeds 17 -> 1) with no newly failing record. The three left differ in steeringTarget on their second frame only (Unity 42.67, Unbound the destination at 50). |
| Walking agents keep the path they were given (Unity's shorten it as they walk) | `AI/DotRecast/DotRecast.Detour.Crowd/DtPathCorridor.cs` (OptimizePathVisibility, OptimizePathTopology), `DtCrowd.cs` (FindCorners, WaitForTopology), `DotRecast.Detour/DtNavMeshQuery.cs` (Raycast straight costs, FinalizeSlicedFindPathPartial), `DtPathUtils.cs` (MergeCorridorStartShortcut), `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (TopologyAgent, TakeSlot, CrowdFilter) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navpath `replan.vis`, `replan.topo`, `replan.budget` all values, `replan.topofar` all 1687 values since Q376 (the path-cost row), `place.moved` walk and `carve.shortcut` now matching for every pose; managed NavMeshAgentReplanTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0 NavMeshAgent autoRepath, isPathStale, pathPending; NavMesh.pathfindingIterationsPerFrame): these concern paths made invalid by NavMesh changes and paths being computed; nothing documents shortening a path while walking. Measured: at every update a walking agent with one corner casts a ray to it, with more to its second corner when the straight way there costs at least 1 and less than the way through the first (the agent's own area mask and costs); a ray that reaches the point replaces the corridor's start with the polygons it crosses up to the last of them the corridor holds (`replan.vis`: across water costing the agent 1, not at 10 or with water masked out; a ray ending on a corner vertex inside a polygon off the corridor changes nothing). Once per update, of all agents, the one that has waited longest (walking with more than two corridor polygons, more than 0.5 s, the first in Unity's slot order of equals: freed slots are reused last first) has its corridor searched again from its first polygon to its last for 8 iterations, used only when the search ends within them, its first 8 polygons up to the farthest corridor polygon reached replacing the corridor's start (`replan.topo`: walkers turn back through the mud one per update; one given its path again at 0.4 s is searched with the others, its wait kept; `replan.topofar`: on a floor of many polygons none turns). These searches are not part of pathfindingIterationsPerFrame (`replan.budget`: at 1 iteration per frame, with another agent waiting many frames for its path, the same walkers turn at the same frames). remainingDistance follows the corners found again from where the agent stands after its step (`replan.topo`: the frame a step passes a corner). Unbound's choices, unmeasured: an agent placed again (Warp, a NavMesh change) keeps its slot and its wait; an agent enabled off the NavMesh takes its slot when first placed; a path request pending during the search starts where it was requested (whether Unity's starts from the shortened corridor is unmeasured). Not separately measured: the 1 cm nearest corner, the least cost of 1, the 32 and 8 polygons kept, and the shortcut's range (corners up to about 13 m away). |

Body curves (RootT/RootQ) of generic clips move a generic Animator through graph outputs as through its controller, with the same Bake Into Pose and Based Upon settings, for clip playables, mixers (inputs summed unnormalized) and controller playables, with builtin root motion, OnAnimatorMove (root motion on or off) and root motion off; graph clip playables apply the clip's loop pose to generic curves whatever the root motion (`rootcurves` graph.*, Unity 6000.0.71f1). A generic Animator without a playing controller of its own, driven by playable graph outputs, now moves by their root motion (predicted, unmeasured): clip playables move the root between their previous and current time (whole loops; nothing for seeks or pauses), mixers sum it by weight, jobs pass it on or replace it with the velocities they set; `deltaPosition`/`deltaRotation`/`velocity`/`angularVelocity` take it, `OnAnimatorMove` runs, else `applyRootMotion` applies it, and the root curves no longer pose the root. Layer mixers and controller playables now carry it to the output too (predicted, unmeasured; every composition rule is Unbound's choice): layers apply in order from no motion and only layers that animate the root (its position or rotation curves, or a root node's motion; a mixer when one of its inputs does) contribute, as a layer's pose overrides only the channels it animates: an override layer of weight w blends the motion so far toward its own by w, an additive layer adds its delta times w, a layer of weight 0, whose mask excludes the root (its empty-path entry) or that does not animate the root contributes nothing; a controller playable carries the controller's own root motion (the computation its streams report). Q91 direct humanoid output root motion is done (`animculling`, 1924 added Editor records, Unity 6000.0.71f1): one full-weight nonmirrored AnimationClipPlayable, default-values stream, valid avatar and no controller or loop override; root-motion off/builtin/OnAnimatorMove with either flag, Manual/GameTime, loop crossings, a second scaled avatar, culling and retained movement replay. Q104 humanoid mixer output root motion is done (`animculling`, 2072 added Editor records, Unity 6000.0.71f1 only through the compatibility profile): one two-input AnimationMixerPlayable feeding that same single-output/no-controller context, compatible nonmirrored clips with complete RootQ curves and foot IK disabled, fixed nonnegative unit-sum input weights; 0/1 and 1/0 endpoint controls and a 0.25/0.75 interior blend, translational/rotational deltas, root/body/bone/pivot data, callbacks, both clocks, zero/successive/loop-crossing samples and visible/culled/return phases. Reuses humanoid sampling/blending/retargeting; normalizes each sampled body rotation before blending, and visibility return projects the retained blended clip step before feet-pivot correction without advancing sampler memory. Humanoid mixer trees are generalised (`animculling` humanMixerShape, 2640 added Editor records with the event-shape controls, Unity 6000.0.71f1 through the same compatibility flag): any input count, nested mixers, one-input mixers, foot IK on the inputs and input weights changed between evaluations, each mixer's weights summing to one, through the same humanoid sampler, blend and feet pivot as a direct clip; the public pivot stays zero while no root motion is requested. Q120 mixer weight sums are done (`animculling` humanMixerShape/genericMixerSum, 2702 added Editor records, Unity 6000.0.71f1; humanoid through the same flag): generic and humanoid inputs sum unnormalized with the default (zero, identity) taking the rest below one, negative weights are stored as 0 and NaN counts as 0, the feet pivot's gravity weight is the clips' mean above one, and a visibility return after weights changed while culled re-blends the retained clip poses and steps with the current weights and re-places the feet pivot (an attached controller keeps the last blend, warn, unmeasured). Humanoid mixer inputs with only some body rotation curves, or none, blend as whole rotations and move the root (tests/projects/humanoid bodyGraph.*, Unity 6000.0.71f1: mixers at 0.35 + 0.65, 0.3 + 0.4, 0.8 + 0.6, three inputs, a mixer in a mixer and a layer mixer; Foot IK off). An AnimatorControllerPlayable with humanoid clips as the source of the Animator's only full-weight output moves the root by its clips' humanoid root motion, as the Animator's own controller does (tests/projects/humanoid controllerGraph.*, Unity 6000.0.71f1). stroke gradients (44 gradient cases, 3 API lines, the context painter's gradient on entry and a panel image): the colour follows the share of the whole path's length drawn so far, all sub-paths in order; a straight segment gets a row of vertices at every gradient key and at least every eighth of its gradient span (at most 17 rows), an arc one colour per ring step, a curve one per piece end after its pieces are cut at the keys; joins and caps take the colour of their segment end; colours are read in 8-bit steps, Fixed and PerceptualBlend modes as the gradient evaluates them (PerceptualBlend unrounded), a gradient in linear colour space shown in sRGB; documented: a gradient overrides strokeColor and null falls back on it; measured: whichever of strokeColor and strokeGradient was set last is used, a transparent strokeColor draws nothing also under a gradient, the gradient is taken as it is when set, the getter answers a copy of the last one set (also after null, Clear and for the next element's context painter, which all turn it off) and a white one before any; `Gradient` key times read back in 16-bit steps and a new gradient's colorSpace is Uninitialized; an arc whose inner side reaches or passes its centre (radius at most half the width plus 2) advances the gradient by half a circle of its radius per vertex, so the gradient is used up within its first vertices (measured, also for ArcTo corners); a curve piece whose ends coincide has no length; unmeasured: more than 16 rows on one segment, gradients on paths shorter than 1e-4; residuals in Known insignificant residuals; curves without length (meshes `gradient-curve-loop*`, `gradient-curve-tiny-at-origin`, `curve-loop-*`, `curve-tiny-elsewhere`, `quadratic-loop`): a curve whose ends coincide (within 0.001) is an arc of radius 0 around that point, drawn with coverage circles between the normals of its end tangents, the same wherever it lies; when those tangents oppose it shrinks to the curve's middle point, where a dot of the line's width is drawn and the joins and bounds take that point; unmeasured: an arc piece of radius > 0 whose end tangents oppose (shrunk the same way), pieces cut at the subdivision depth limit (drawn as their chord); ExpandItem/CollapseItem report even when the state does not change (an item without children does nothing); toggle presses expand or collapse one item, a row pressed with Alt its whole subtree; right/left navigation expands or collapses the selected items, else moves to the first child, parent or next item with children; selection by id expands the item's collapsed ancestors (each reported), not the item itself, unlike the Remarks' "will also expand the selected item(s)" (measured with an item with children); SetRootItems reports itemsSourceChanged, drops every expanded state (measured: an expanded id given again is collapsed) and the rows follow at the next refresh (the same list given again is read again); Move leaves the parents' TreeViewItemData children as they were, as in Unity; rows (indent, Foldout-style toggle, content) named after their classes; tree data (tree-data section): Move as documented (parent -1 the root, child index -1 the last child) and measured (a child index past the end is the last child; an item moved after itself among its siblings counts without itself; into itself, under its own descendant or with an unknown id nothing happens; a parent id that is not in the tree throws ArgumentNullException before anything moves, a child index below -1 throws IndexOutOfRangeException after the item went to the end of its new parent; the selection follows the moved item and is reported again; `rebuildTree` false leaves the view's rows as they were until the next refresh), AddItem (the parent's TreeViewItemData gets the child too; a parent id that is not in the tree adds at the root; an id already in the tree adds a second item, and the id then names the new one, each row keeping its own data; root items that give an id twice do the same; a parent whose data is null does not get the child in its TreeViewItemData; an item added at a child index shows in the rows at once, also without a rebuild) and TryRemoveItem (the parent's TreeViewItemData loses the child, descendants go with it); a move among siblings to a child index below -1 throws and moves nothing (tree-controls section); the rows and GetAllItemIds are a view of the hierarchy as of its last refresh (a move, an expansion, new root items or the tree view's refresh), so items added or removed without a rebuild show there later while parents and children answer at once; tree-drag and tree-drag-positions sections, every pointer position's parent and child index as dragAndDropUpdate and handleDrop see them, the hover bar and its markers, every callback): a drop onto a row adds the dragged items as its last children and expands it; a drop between rows goes under the parent and at the child index given by the nearest rows that are not dragged: any depth from the next row's (0 below the last row) to the previous row's (one more when it is expanded with children), picked by the pointer's x in indent steps after the toggle when several are possible; a drop before the first row is first at the root, and one with no undragged row before it last at the root; several items move in tree order (an item's own child sorts before it when both are dragged, as measured) and keep that order; the view refreshes once, then itemIndexChanged reports each item whose parent or child index changed; an item dropped onto its own descendant moves nothing; a collapsed row hovered for 700 ms (panel clock) with the pointer within 10 points expands, and the end of the drag collapses every item that was not expanded before that (each item with children reports a collapse); while the pointer keeps resting after such an expansion, a collapsed item 0 with children expands after another 700 ms although it is not hovered (measured, tree-controls section, and reproduced); `reorderable` false (the documented default) and canStartDrag false refuse the drag after the pressed row is selected, handleDrop Rejected refuses the drop; ### Assets, import, networking

Done: texture import (sprites, NPOT, max size, alpha, cubemaps, sRGB/normal maps), audio import
settings, model scale/blend shapes/generic rigs, AssetBundles, Addressables (AssetDatabase mode),
ScriptedImporters, UnityWebRequest core (multipart, certificates, timeouts, file, texture).

| `generateSecondaryUV` (lightmap UVs) | `crates/unbound-assets/src/model.rs`, `model/lightmap_uv.rs`, `model/lightmap_uv/` | S | partial: reproduced for connected parts of any size, charts that touch themselves at a vertex, charts that overlap themselves, and degenerate and non-manifold geometry (tests/projects/lightmapuvs, Unity 6000.0.71f1: UV2 per triangle corner and the vertex count match exactly for Cube, Multi, Pieces, Sphere (720 triangles), Ico (320), Barrel (1008), Rock (5120), Compound, Twins and Cylinder (a large part after another part of one mesh) in all eight variants, Grid and TwoUV in both UV2 and vertex count after normal normalization and attribute splitting; the Torus matches as well). The importer builds the unwrap's input as the Editor is inferred to hold the mesh (control points in file order, an OBJ's in first-use order, equal positions welded; triangles in face order, each its own source; UV0 per corner), derives the pack margin (manual, or calculated from the mesh area, resolution and object scale; the packer halves a margin too large for the charts), then fits the mesh into a unit cube and welds it, segments each connected part into charts (a part of more than 100 triangles first on a copy simplified to a tenth by quadric-ordered vertex collapses, then refined back in three steps, with the Editor's numbering of the coarse facets by position in the part), lays each chart flat from corrected angles by least squares (a vertex where the chart meets itself split per fan, the corners keeping the first fan's UV), cuts a chart that overlaps itself into connected pieces, packs the charts, and splits vertices on the generated UV2. Degenerate triangles are removed before the unwrap and take the UV2 of a neighbour's corners, non-manifold edges and vertices are split, and a mesh with no triangle left is imported empty, as in the Editor (the Repair variant: twelve models match; the empty mesh is measured for one mesh of one sub-mesh). Open: equal-sized charts can be packed in each other's places (Organic at hard angle 30: 4 single-triangle charts, 12 corners up to 0.034 apart; studied and left: the swapped charts' boxes are equal in width and one or two single-precision steps apart in height here, so their order in the packer follows the last step of the box computation; sorting the boxes another way or comparing them in single precision matched fewer recorded meshes; the charts trade places and the lightmap is used the same); with `weldVertices` off the unwrap gets every point as its own (lightmapuvs NoWeld: 14 of 15 models match by index, Organic has one more packing tie of two single-triangle charts, 6 values 0.067 apart; NoWeldOff and NoWeldRaw match in all 30; measured on FBX, an OBJ's points come in first-use order and are not measured without welding); other unwrap failures (charts that do not fit, positions that are not finite) still warn and keep the file's UV2; not measured: the empty import with mesh optimization off, several sub-meshes, blend shapes or skinning, and a mesh smaller than 1e-6 (it welds to nothing and is imported empty as well). The polygon order looks at every remaining triangle of the vertices in the cache, so its time grows with the square of the triangles around one vertex: a fan of a hundred thousand triangles takes seconds. A Polygon-mode sprite's mesh is always Tight (mesh type and the 32-pixel minimum do not apply), and its default physics shape is one path through the vertices of its mesh's boundary edges, in the order of an edge hash table whose shape reproduces every Polygon control: separate islands and holes follow each other in that one path; a zero-area outline renders the quad and takes its boundary (a texture without traceable alpha or a rect outside the texture take the same quad, unmeasured). Thirteen Polygon controls (concave with an off-centre pivot, islands, hole, 60 vertices, two loops of 160 edges that pin that table's shape, custom and disabled shapes, Full Rect, under 32 pixels, no outline, zero-area outline). Runtime `Sprite.Create` traces the texture as the importer does (tests/projects/spriteoutlines, 5,339 values, zero differences; independent Native repeats identical): the Tight mesh and texture rect (a rect under 32 pixels on a side stays a quad; extrude 0 and 1 trace alike), and with `generateFallbackPhysicsShape` the traced physics shape; readable and unreadable imported textures alike, and the texels the CPU holds when a script wrote them: a new texture before and after `Apply`, one `Apply(false, true)` made non-readable, an imported readable texture rewritten by `SetPixels32` (applied or not). Only the rect's texels are handed to the trace; an imported texture nothing rewrote is traced from its import (floating-point textures unmeasured: an imported one is not traced, one a script made is traced from its 8-bit reading). A PolygonCollider2D added in Play mode to a sprite's object takes the sprite's physics shape, else the outline a generated physics shape would have (also for Full Rect sprites; holes give extra paths); a TilemapCollider2D's sprite tiles take the same outline (convex outlines measured; the decomposition of an outline with holes into tile shapes is not part of the probe). `UnityEditor.Sprites.SpriteUtility.GenerateOutline` traces a texture rect with its detail, alpha tolerance and hole detection, in pixels from the rect's centre, and `Sprite.OverridePhysicsShape` takes paths in rect space to units from the pivot (a null path throws `ArgumentNullException` and one under 3 points `ArgumentException`, before any path is set; no paths clear the shape; `GenerateOutline` of no texture throws `ArgumentNullException`); the SVG importer's generated physics shape uses both (tests/projects/svgsprites). Not covered: `SpriteUtility.GenerateOutlineFromSprite` (the PSD and Aseprite importers' call, still a stub), BoxCollider2D/CircleCollider2D fitting to a sprite when added, the SpriteRenderer's flip on an added collider. |

## Order of attack

1. **Make gaps loud** (the tooling list above). After it, this file is regenerated from
   `api-check`/`doctor` output instead of audits.
2. ~~**Messages and the player loop**~~: done (tests/projects/messages): `OnMouse*`,
   focus/pause, `OnBeforeTransformParentChanged`, frustum culling with
   `isVisible`/`OnBecameInvisible`, `ShadowsOnly`/TwoSided, the FixedUpdate cap, cursor
   lock/visibility, DontSave roots, coroutines on disabled behaviours, `Debug.Break`,
   `Application.Quit`.
3. **Built-in RP correctness**: ~~depth/depth-normals/motion-vector textures with their camera
   events, main material properties from shader attributes, disabled passes, transparency sort
   axis, `.renderTexture` assets, baked reflection probes~~ (done: tests/projects/builtinrp,
   baked, display, materials), ~~Projector, replacement shaders, oblique matrices with inverted
   culling, cubemap renders, realtime probes, deferred and vertex-lit paths~~ (done:
   tests/projects/builtinfx); SpriteMask and tiled sprites, texture mip chains and formats (one
   protocol change).
4. **Animation and audio**: one root-motion pass with a probe; mixer effects and filter
   components (one DSP chain); constraints; polar 2D blend trees; then humanoid avatars (large).
5. **Physics**: ~~2D joints~~ (done: tests/projects/joints2d), effectors, particle vertex streams and
   collision events, NavMeshObstacle, project defaults and DynamicsManager settings, local
   physics scenes, raycast UVs.
6. **UI and text**: ~~USS transitions~~ (done: tests/projects/uitransitions), per-draw `_ClipRect`, ~~Unity's built-in UI sprites and GameSkin
   from its resource files~~ (done: tests/projects/imguiskin), ~~input events within a frame~~ (done: tests/projects/inputframes), clipboard, IME,
   ~~TextMesh~~ (done: tests/projects/textmesh and glyphraster, dynamic font texture packing included).
7. **Assets and networking**: FBX materials, LOD names, lightmap UVs, AssetBundle downloads and
   caching, audio downloads, ~~`InstantiateAsync`~~ (done: tests/projects/instantiateasync).

Deferred by decision (see ROADMAP): VFX Graph, XR, LOD cross-fades, Terrain leftovers, console
codecs.

Q64/Q221/Q287: instance multipliers on CPU-expanded mesh instances: done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 on OpenGL Core (tests/projects/cmdbuffers `device.multiplier.mesh.*`, `placed.*`, `camera.*`; NVIDIA only). The Editor multiplies a mesh-instancing call's GPU instance count (the Scripting API: "multiply the instance count of every draw call"), through command buffers and a camera's queued `Graphics.RenderMeshInstanced` and `DrawMeshInstanced`; the added IDs n to n·m − 1 have `unity_InstanceID` equal to the raw ID and read the instance data past the call's elements. Unbound adds them after the call's last instance with zero matrices and instanced properties, which is what the Editor reads where no earlier draw wrote there, and with the last instance's winding (Unbound's choice). Measured, not reproduced: where earlier instanced draws wrote more elements, the Editor's added instances read those (data left in its instance buffer, `device.multiplier.stale.colors.2`, kept as a visible difference); Unbound warns once. Unmeasured: other per-instance built-ins of the added instances (LOD fade, probes, lightmap scale/offset keep the draw's values), standalone instancing whose multiplied IDs pass a native batch (warned, not added), stereo instancing keywords (XR deferred).


Q274: compute shaders keep predefined sampler states on the measured 6000.0.71f1 OpenGL Core
device (`tests/projects/renderapi` `computeSamplers.*`). Texture-named samplers continue to use
the texture's filter and wrap settings, including `volumeCompute.*.point`; the graphics-program
inline sampler observation does not apply to compute kernels. The URP `upscale_stp` comparison
is retained by `tests/projects/renderapi/stp.py`.

Q221 shader-declarations replies preserve a reason and retry flag for opaque per-pass variant choices, including serialized built-ins and generated surface passes. A known empty choice list remains distinct from an unreadable one; managed caching retries transient per-pass inputs and accepts replies from older hosts. Provider and managed metadata tests cover these transport guarantees.

### Q402/Q417 residual scope after the measured scripting fixes

The Unity 6000.0.71f1 references remain strict. Serialized prefab metadata
passes all 6280 field comparisons (18 former field differences closed), with
serializedstructure 100/100 and serializedbindings 155/155. The existing
callback-order differences and exact scene-event timing differences remain raw;
they do not establish a documented order beyond the guarantees recorded by
editorcallbacks. No callback ordering or tolerance was changed here.
TextAsset payload accounting closes four appfacts records (525/555, previously
521/555). Scripted-import leaf identity passes 136/136, alongside the unchanged
184/184 scriptedfileids reference. These counts cover their measured contexts.

Metadata queries produce no notification; a process-local host notification does. No synthetic first-key rule. |

Fixed per-type byte constants are contradicted by the controls. |

All five follow-ups retain their raw failures; none is waived by a broader
passing suite. The new memory and hierarchy diagnostics are supplemental
